The Global Existence of Martingale Solutions to Stochastic Compressible Navier-Stokes Equations with Density-dependent Viscosity
Yachun Li, Lizhen Zhang

Abstract
. The global existence of martingale solutions to the compressible Navier-Stokes equations driven by stochastic external forces, with density-dependent viscosity and vacuum, is established in this paper. This work can be regarded as a stochastic version of the deterministic Navier-Stokes equations [ 38 ] (Vasseur-Yu, Invent. Math., 206:935–974, 2016.), in which the global existence of weak solutions was established for adiabatic exponent γ > 1 \gamma>1 . For the stochastic case, the regularity of density and velocity is even worse for passing the limit in nonlinear terms. We design a regularized system to approximate the original system. To make up for the lack of regularity of velocity, we need to add an artificial Rayleigh damping term besides the artificial viscosity and damping forces in [ 39 , 38 ] . Moreover, we have to send the artificial terms to 0 0 in a different order.



2010 Mathematics Subject Classification
35A01, 35Q30, 35Q35, 35Q40, 35R60, 60H15.

Acknowledgements.
The authors’ research was supported in part
by Chinese National Natural Science Foundation under grants 12371221, 12161141004, and 11831011. The authors were also grateful to the supports by Shanghai Frontiers Science Center of Modern Analysis and the Fundamental Research Funds for the Central Universities. The authors appreciate Prof. Hermano Frid and Prof. Deng Zhang for valuable discussion.

1.
Introduction

Compressible Navier-Stokes equations describe the motion of compressible viscous Newtonian fluid. Practically, besides the structural vibration of the fluid, the fluid will also be affected by random external forces such as humidity, wind, solar radiation, industrial pollution, etc. Therefore it is reasonable to add stochastic forces to Navier-Stokes equations. The system of stochastic compressible Navier-Stokes equations (SCNS for short) in torus
\mathbb{T}^{3}
reads as

(1.1)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\rho_{t}+\operatorname{div}\left(\rho\mathbf{u}\right)=0,\\
\operatorname{d}(\rho\mathbf{u})+\left(\operatorname{div}\left(\rho\mathbf{u}\otimes\mathbf{u}\right)+\nabla p-\operatorname{div}\mathcal{T}\right)\operatorname{d}t=\rho\mathbb{F}(\rho,\mathbf{u})\operatorname{d}W,\end{array}\right.

where
(t,x)\in\mathbb{R}^{+}\times\mathbb{T}^{3}
,
\rho
is the density,
\mathbf{u}=(u_{1},u_{2},u_{3})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{3}
denotes the velocity,
p
is the pressure. For polytropic isentropic gas, the pressure can be expressed as
\displaystyle p=a\rho^{\gamma}
, where
a
is a constant.
\mathcal{T}=2\mu\mathbb{D}(\mathbf{u})+\lambda\operatorname{div}\mathbf{u}\mathbb{I}_{3}
is the viscous stress tensor,
\mu>0
is the shear viscosity coefficient,
\lambda+\frac{2}{3}\mu>0
is the bulk viscosity coefficient,
\mathbb{I}_{3}
is the
3\times 3
identity matrix,

(1.2)

\mathbb{D}\mathbf{u}=\frac{\nabla\mathbf{u}+\nabla\mathbf{u}^{\top}}{2}

is the deformation tensor.

\rho\mathbb{F}\left(\rho,\mathbf{u}\right)\operatorname{d}W
is the external multiplicative noise.
W
is a cylindrical
\mathcal{F}_{t}
-adapted Wiener process in the stochastic basis
\left(\Omega,\mathcal{F},\mathbb{P}\right)
,
\left(\mathcal{F}_{t}\right)_{t\geqslant 0}
is the right-continuous filtration, see §
5.1
in Appendix.

(1.3)

W=\sum\limits_{k=1}^{+\infty}e_{k}\beta_{k},\quad\operatorname{d}W=\sum\limits_{k=1}^{+\infty}e_{k}\operatorname{d}\beta_{k},

where
\beta_{k}
is the standard real Brownian motion,
\{e_{k}\}_{k=1}^{+\infty}
is an orthonormal basis in an auxiliary separable Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}
, which is isometrically isomorphic to
l^{2}
, the space of square-summable sequences.
\mathcal{H}
is independent of domain
\mathbb{T}^{3}
.
Let
H
be a Bochner space,
\mathbb{F}\left(\rho,\mathbf{u}\right)
is an
H
-valued operator from
\mathcal{H}
to
\mathcal{H}
. Denoting the inner product in
\mathcal{H}
as
\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle
,
the inner product

(1.4)

\langle\mathbb{F}\left(\rho,\mathbf{u}\right),e_{k}\rangle=\mathbf{F}_{k}\left(t,x,\rho,\mathbf{u}\right)

is a 3D
H
-valued vector function, which shows the strength of the external stochastic forces,

(1.5)

\mathbb{F}\left(\rho,\mathbf{u}\right)\operatorname{d}W=\sum\limits_{k=1}^{+\infty}\mathbf{F}_{k}\left(t,x,\rho,\mathbf{u}\right)\operatorname{d}\beta_{k},\quad\mathbb{F}\left(\rho,\mathbf{u}\right)=\sum\limits_{k=1}^{+\infty}\mathbf{F}_{k}\left(t,x,\rho,\mathbf{u}\right)e_{k}.

We will write
\mathbf{F}_{k}\left(t,x,\rho,\mathbf{u}\right)
as
\mathbf{F}_{k}\left(\rho,\mathbf{u}\right)
for short in the sequel.
Besides,
\rho
and
\mathbf{u}
are actually random variables depending on
\omega\in\Omega
:
\rho=\rho(\omega,t,x)
,
\mathbf{u}=\mathbf{u}(\omega,t,x)
, for
\omega\in\Omega
; however, we write
\rho(t,x)
and
\mathbf{u}(t,x)
for short to make the notations consistent with the usual deterministic studies in fluid dynamic equations.

1.1.
Some progresses

When
\rho\mathbb{F}(\rho,\mathbf{u})\equiv 0
, system (
1.1
) reduces to deterministic Navier-Stokes system. There are numerous results concerning the well-posedness of weak solutions, among which we select in this subsection the ones most relevant to our work. Kazhikhov-Shelukhin
[
29
]

obtained the existence of global weak solutions to the initial boundary problem of 1D Navier-Stokes-Fourier system in 1977. Lions
[
31
]
proved the global existence with large initial data in 1998 for
\gamma\geqslant\frac{3}{2}
as
n=2
and
\gamma\geqslant\frac{9}{5}
as
n=3
. Then in 2001, Feireisl-Novotný-Petzeltová
[
17
]
extended the range of adiabatic exponent to
\gamma>\frac{n}{2}
in Leray sense. In 2003, Jiang-Zhang
[
25
]
extended to
\gamma>1
for the spherically symmetric solutions. In 2021, Hu
[
21
]
obtained the renormalized global weak solutions for
\frac{6}{5}<\gamma<\frac{3}{2}
up to a closed set with zero parabolic Hausdorff measure.

The above results are all for the case of constant viscosities.
However, the viscosity coefficients
\mu
and
\lambda
may depend on the temperature or the density, or both, which can be mathematically derived from the Chapman-Enskorg expansion in Boltzman equation
[
30
]
. Physically, one can also find this dependence in the inverse cut-off power law models and Maxwellian molecules
[
11
]
. In the isentropic case, by Boyle law and Gay-Lussac law, such dependence is reduced to the dependence on the density, denoted as
\mu(\rho)
and
\lambda(\rho)
. In 1995, Kazhikhov-Vaigant
[
28
]
considered the global existence of solutions to Navier-Stokes-Fourier system for the 2D case with the viscosity coefficient satisfying
\mu\left(\rho\right)=1
,
\lambda\left(\rho\right)=\rho^{\beta}
,
\beta>3
.
In 2006, Bresch and Desjardins
[
7
]
first introduced Bresch-Desjardins entropy to get further more regularity of density so as to solve the shallow water problem, in which the Bresch-Desjardins relation (B-D relation)
\lambda(\rho)=2(\rho\mu^{\prime}(\rho)-\mu(\rho))
is necessary to balance the terms in deducing the B-D entropy. One year later, Mellet-Vasseur
[
32
]
deduced the Mellet-Vasseur type inequality which shows that
\rho\mathbf{u}^{2}
is bounded in
L^{\infty}\left(0,T;L\log L\left(\mathbb{T}^{3}\right)\right)
, and established the compactness of
\sqrt{\rho}\mathbf{u}
in
L^{2}([0,T]\times\mathbb{T}^{3})
.
In 2016 Vasseur-Yu
[
38
]
proved the global existence when the viscosity coefficients satisfy
\mu(\rho)=\frac{\rho}{2}
,
\lambda(\rho)=0
without extra constraint on the range of adiabatic exponent
\gamma
by means of vanishing viscosity method, the Bresch-Desjardins entropy (B-D entropy) and Mellet-Vasseur type inequality.

Now we turn to the stochastic case:
\rho\mathbb{F}(\rho,\mathbf{u})\not\equiv 0
. We consider the so-called martingale solutions of the system, which can be interpreted as weak solutions in the stochastic version
[
36
,
27
]
. The existence of 2D periodic martingale solutions to Navier-Stokes-Fourier equations was first studied by Tornatore
[
37
]
for Cauchy problem in 2000. In 2013, Feireisl-Malowski-Notovný
[
15
]
get the global existence for the 3D Dirichlet problem with the same stochastic force
\rho\operatorname{d}W
in
[
37
]
. For multiplicative noise, Wang-Wang
[
40
]
obtained the global martingale solutions in a bounded domain with non-slip boundary conditions in 2015. In 2016, Breit-Hofmanová
[
6
]
showed the existence of global martingale solutions on a torus with periodic boundary conditions.
In 2017, Smith
[
35
]
got the global martingale solutions with Lipschitz continuous stochastic force. There are also results on singular limit
[
2
]
, the stationary solutions
[
5
]
, and the weak-strong uniqueness
[
3
]
, see also
[
16
]
. The above results are all for the case of constant viscosity coefficients except in
[
37
]
where the case
\lambda(\rho)=1+\rho^{\beta}
was considered but no degeneracy is involved.
In 2020, Breit-Feireisl
[
1
]
considered the case with temperature-dependent viscosities but no degeneracy:
\underline{\mu}\cdot\left(1+\theta\right)<\mu(\theta)<\bar{\mu}\cdot\left(1+\theta\right)
, where
\underline{\mu}
and
\bar{\mu}
are specific positive constants, they proved the existence of martingale solution to Navier-Stokes-Fourier equations. Very recently in 2022, for the density-dependent viscosities same as in
[
38
]
, Brzeźniak-Dhariwal-Zatorska
[
8
]
proved the sequential stability of the martingale solutions to (
1.1
) with density-dependent viscosities, based on the assumption that there exist global martingale solutions. Our purpose in this paper is to establish the global existence of martingale solutions.

1.2.
Our main result

We consider the stochastic isentropic compressible Navier-Stokes equations with density-dependent viscosities. Once the viscosity coefficients depend on density,
\operatorname{d}\left(\rho\mathbf{u}\right)
will degenerate at vacuum
\rho(t,x)=0
, which causes the uncertainty of the evolution of velocity
\operatorname{d}\mathbf{u}
. Moreover,
\operatorname{div}\mathcal{T}
also degenerates at vacuum, which has an influence on the regularity of
\mathbf{u}
. Like
[
38
]
, we consider

(1.6)

\mu(\rho)=\frac{\rho}{2},\quad\lambda(\rho)=0,

which satisfies the B-D relation
\lambda(\rho)=2(\rho\mu^{\prime}(\rho)-\mu(\rho))
.
With (
1.6
), (
1.1
) turns into

(1.7)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\vskip 5.16663pt\rho_{t}+\operatorname{div}\left(\rho\mathbf{u}\right)=0,\\
\operatorname{d}\left(\rho\mathbf{u}\right)+\left(\operatorname{div}\left(\rho\mathbf{u}\otimes\mathbf{u}\right)+\nabla p-\operatorname{div}(\rho\mathbb{D}\mathbf{u})\right)\operatorname{d}t=\rho\mathbb{F}\left(\rho,\mathbf{u}\right)\operatorname{d}W.\\
\end{array}\right.

We aim at the global existence of martingale solutions to (
1.7
) in torus
\mathbb{T}^{3}
, given the initial conditions

(1.8)

\rho|_{t=0}=\rho_{0},\quad\rho\mathbf{u}|_{t=0}=\mathbf{q}_{0}.

We assume that there exists
f=\left(f_{1},f_{2},\cdots,f_{k},\cdots\right)
, the constant
f_{k}\geqslant 0
, such that

(1.9)

\displaystyle\left|\rho\mathbf{F}_{k}\left(\rho,\mathbf{u}\right)\right|\leqslant f_{k}\cdot\left(|\rho|+|\rho\mathbf{u}|\right),\quad\sum\limits_{k=1}^{+\infty}f_{k}^{2}<+\infty.

When
\rho=0
, we define
\mathbf{F}_{k}\left(\rho,\mathbf{u}\right)=0
, when
\rho>0
, we write
\mathbf{F}_{k}\left(\rho,\mathbf{u}\right)=\mathbf{F}_{k}\left(\rho,\frac{\rho\mathbf{u}}{\rho}\right)
,
\mathbf{q}=\rho\mathbf{u}
.

We first give the definition of martingale solutions.
Let
\Lambda
be the law (see §
5.1
in Appendix) of
\rho_{0}
and
\mathbf{q}_{0}
, i.e.
\Lambda=\mathcal{L}\left[\rho_{0},\mathbf{q}_{0}\right]
.

Definition 1.1
.

\left(\left(\Omega,\mathcal{F},\mathbb{P}\right),\rho,\mathbf{u},W\right)
is called a martingale solution to Cauchy problem (
1.7
)-(
1.8
) with initial law
\Lambda
, where
\left(\rho,\mathbf{u}\right)
is defined in probability space
\left(\Omega,\mathcal{F},\mathbb{P}\right)
, if

(1)

\left(\Omega,\mathcal{F},\mathbb{P}\right)
is a stochastic basis with a complete right-continuous filtration
\mathcal{F}=\left(\mathcal{F}_{t}\right)_{t\geq 0}
;

(2)

the density
\rho
and the velocity
\mathbf{u}
are stochastic processes adapted to
\mathcal{F}
;

(3)

\mathcal{L}\left[\rho(0,x),\mathbf{q}(0,x)\right]=\Lambda
;

(4)

the equation of continuity

(1.10)

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{T}\partial_{t}\varphi(t)\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\rho\psi(x)\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}t=\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\varphi(0,x)\rho(0,x)\operatorname{d}x+\int_{0}^{T}\varphi(t)\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\rho\mathbf{u}\cdot\nabla\psi(x)\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}t,

holds
\mathbb{P}

a.s.
for all
\varphi(t)\in C_{c}^{\infty}\left([0,T)\right)
, and all
\psi(x)\in C^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{T}^{3}\right)
;

(5)

the momentum equation

\displaystyle-\varphi(0)\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\mathbf{q}(0,x)\cdot\psi(x)\operatorname{d}x-\int_{0}^{T}\varphi_{t}(t)\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\rho\mathbf{u}\psi(x)\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}t

(1.11)

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{T}\varphi(t)\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\rho\mathbf{u}\otimes\mathbf{u}:\nabla\psi(x)\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}t-\int_{0}^{T}\varphi(t)\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\rho^{\gamma}\operatorname{div}\psi(x)\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}t

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{T}\varphi(t)\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\rho\mathbb{D}\mathbf{u}:\nabla\psi(x)\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}t=\int_{0}^{T}\varphi(t)\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\rho\mathbb{F}(\rho,\mathbf{u})\psi(x)\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}W,

holds
\mathbb{P}

a.s.
for all
\varphi(t)\in C_{c}^{\infty}\left([0,T)\right)
, and all
\psi(x)\in C^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{T}^{3}\right)
.

For the convenience of stating our main theorem, we introduce the notation of the energy

(1.12)

e(t)=\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\left(\frac{1}{2}\rho|\mathbf{u}|^{2}+\frac{a}{\gamma}\rho^{\gamma}\operatorname{d}z\right)\operatorname{d}x=\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\left(\frac{1}{2}\frac{|\mathbf{q}|^{2}}{\rho}+\frac{a}{\gamma}\rho^{\gamma}\operatorname{d}z\right)\operatorname{d}x,

the B-D entropy

\displaystyle\tilde{e}(t)=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\left(\frac{1}{2}\rho\left|\mathbf{u}+\nabla\ln\rho\right|^{2}+\frac{a}{\gamma}\rho^{\gamma}\right)\operatorname{d}x

(1.13)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\left(\frac{1}{2}\frac{|\mathbf{q}|^{2}}{\rho}+\left|\mathbf{q}\cdot\nabla\ln\rho\right|+\frac{1}{2}\rho\left|\nabla\ln\rho\right|^{2}+\frac{a}{\gamma}\rho^{\gamma}\right)\operatorname{d}x,

and the Mellet-Vasseur quantity

(1.14)

\displaystyle\tilde{\tilde{e}}(t)=\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\rho\left(1+\frac{\left|\mathbf{q}\right|^{2}}{\rho^{2}}\right)\ln\left(1+\frac{\left|\mathbf{q}\right|^{2}}{\rho^{2}}\right)\operatorname{d}x.

Our main theorem is as follows.

Theorem 1.1
.

Let
\gamma>1
. If there exists a positive constant
C
, such that, for some constant
r>4
,

(1.15)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[e(0)^{r}\right]\leqslant C,\quad\mathbb{E}\left[\tilde{e}(0)^{r}\right]\leqslant C,\quad\mathbb{E}\left[\tilde{\tilde{e}}(0)^{r}\right]\leqslant C,

and

(1.16)

\Lambda\left(\left\{\left.\omega\in\Omega\right|\rho_{0}(x)\geqslant 0\right\}\right)=1,\quad\Lambda\left(\left\{\omega\in\Omega\left|0<\underline{m}\leqslant\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\rho_{0}(x)\operatorname{d}x\leqslant\overline{m}<\infty\right.\right\}\right)=1,

where
\underline{m}
and
\overline{m}
are constants, then there exists a martingale solution
\left(\left(\Omega,\mathcal{F},\mathbb{P}\right),\rho,\mathbf{u},W\right)
to (
1.7
) in a completed probability space
\left(\Omega,\mathcal{F},\mathbb{P}\right)
. Moreover, there hold

(1)

the energy inequality

(1.17)

\mathbb{E}\left[\sup\limits_{t\in[0,T]}e(t)^{r}\right]\leqslant C(1+\mathbb{E}\left[e(0)^{r}\right]),

where the constant
C>0
depends on
r
,
T
, and
\sum\limits_{k=1}^{+\infty}f^{2}_{k}
;

(2)

the B-D entropy estimates:

(1.18)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sup\limits_{t\in[0,T]}\tilde{e}(t)^{r}\right]\leqslant C\left(1+\mathbb{E}\left[\tilde{e}(0)^{r}\right]\right),

where the constant
C>0
depends on
r,T
,
\sum\limits_{k=1}^{+\infty}f^{2}_{k}
and the initial data;

(3)

the stochastic Mellet-Vasseur inequality:

(1.19)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sup\limits_{t\in[0,T]}\left(\tilde{\tilde{e}}(t)\right)^{r}\right]\leqslant C\left(1+\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\tilde{\tilde{e}}(0)\right)^{r}\right]\right),

where the constant
C>0
depends on
r,T
,
\sum\limits_{k=1}^{+\infty}f^{2}_{k}
and the initial data.

Our proof is based on the vanishing viscosity method. We regularize the original system to construct approximate solutions with better regularity as follows:

(1.20)

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \vskip 5.16663pt&\rho_{t}+\operatorname{div}(\rho\mathbf{u})=\varepsilon\triangle\rho,\\
&\operatorname{d}(\rho\mathbf{u})+\left(\operatorname{div}\left(\rho\mathbf{u}\otimes\mathbf{u}\right)+\nabla p-\operatorname{div}\left(\rho\mathbb{D}\mathbf{u}\right)+\varepsilon\triangle^{2}\mathbf{u}+r_{0}|\mathbf{u}|^{2}\mathbf{u}+r_{1}\rho|\mathbf{u}|^{2}\mathbf{u}+r_{2}\mathbf{u}\right)\operatorname{d}t\\
&=\left(\delta\rho\nabla\triangle^{9}\rho+\eta\rho^{-10}+\kappa\rho\left(\nabla\left(\frac{\triangle\sqrt{\rho}}{\sqrt{\rho}}\right)\right)\right)\operatorname{d}t+\rho\mathbb{F}\left(\rho,\mathbf{u}\right)\operatorname{d}W,\\
\end{aligned}\right.

where
\varepsilon\triangle\rho
is artificial viscosity,
r_{0}|\mathbf{u}|^{2}\mathbf{u}
is Rayleigh damping force,
r_{1}\rho|\mathbf{u}|^{2}\mathbf{u}+r_{2}\mathbf{u}
are the drag forces,
\delta\rho\nabla\triangle^{9}\rho+\eta\rho^{-10}
are artificial pressures,
\kappa\rho\left(\nabla\left(\frac{\triangle\sqrt{\rho}}{\sqrt{\rho}}\right)\right)
is the quantum term.
The initial conditions are
\rho(t,x)|_{t=0}=\rho_{0}(x),\left(\rho\mathbf{u}\right)(t,x)|_{t=0}=\mathbf{q}_{0}(x)
. By Galerkin approximation, we project (
1.20
) in
m
D approximated space
H_{m}
and get the global existence of approximated solutions in
H_{m}
. Employing Burkholder-Davis-Gundy’s inequality, we estimate the
r
-th moment of the stochastic term. Then we do the energy estimates by Itô’s formula so as to figure out the tightness of the laws of approximated solutions
\Pi_{m}\left(\rho\right)
and
\Pi_{m}\left(\rho\mathbf{u}\right)
. By Jakubowski’s extension of Skorokhod’s representation theorem and the energy estimates, we pass to the limit
m\rightarrow+\infty
to obtain the global existence of martingale solutions of (
1.20
). It is worth mentioning that some estimates of the higher order derivatives of density are not uniform in
\varepsilon
. So we would establish the stochastic B-D entropy estimates uniformly in
\varepsilon
. More specifically, the stochastic B-D entropy estimates give the
H^{1}
regularity of
\rho^{\frac{1}{2}}
. Then we vanish the artificial viscosity by sending
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
. By Itô’s formula, passing to the limit
\kappa\rightarrow 0
, we derive the Mellet-Vasseur type inequality in stochastic version. The Mellet-Vasseur’s inequality gives the strong convergence of
\rho^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{u}
, so that the convergence of the nonlinear term
\rho\mathbf{u}\otimes\mathbf{u}
holds
\mathbb{P}
a.s. Let
n\rightarrow\infty
,
\delta\rightarrow 0
,
\eta\rightarrow 0
,
r_{0}\rightarrow 0
at the same time, where
n
is the truncation of
\mathbf{u}
, with
|\mathbf{u}|\leqslant n
. Finally, we take the limit
r_{1}\rightarrow 0
and
r_{2}\rightarrow 0
, then we obtain the global existence of martingale solutions of (
1.7
) for
\gamma>1
, i.e., Theorem
1.1
.
In summary, the order of taking these limits is as follows:

(1)

The artificial viscosity
\varepsilon\triangle\rho
vanishes, i.e.,
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
.

(2)

The quantum term
\kappa\rho\left(\nabla\left(\frac{\triangle\sqrt{\rho}}{\sqrt{\rho}}\right)\right)
vanishes, i.e.,
\kappa\rightarrow 0
.

(3)

The artificial pressure terms
\delta\rho\nabla\triangle^{9}\rho
and
\eta\rho^{-10}
vanish at the same time with the Rayleigh damping term
r_{0}|\mathbf{u}|^{2}\mathbf{u}
, i.e.,
\delta\rightarrow 0
,
\eta\rightarrow 0
,
r_{0}\rightarrow 0
,
n\rightarrow\infty
at the same time, where
n
is the truncation of
\mathbf{u}
,
|\mathbf{u}|\leqslant n
.

(4)

The artificial damping terms
r_{1}\rho|\mathbf{u}|^{2}\mathbf{u}
and
r_{2}\mathbf{u}
vanish, i.e.,
r_{1}\rightarrow 0
and
r_{2}\rightarrow 0
.

The difficulties and the strategies for our problem are analyzed as follows.

1.

Loss of regularity of
\rho\mathbf{u}
with respect to time
t
caused by the stochastic external forces.
The Gaussian process in the right-hand side of the
(\ref{sto NS system})_{2}
is at most Hölder-
\frac{1}{2}
continuous. Moreover, the Aubin-Lions’ lemma does not apply to
\rho\mathbf{u}
in this case either. Therefore, we should consider the time continuity of
\rho\mathbf{u}
so as to obtain the compactness of
\rho\mathbf{u}
in
C\left([0,T];L^{\frac{3}{2}}\left(\mathbb{T}^{3}\right)\right)
. Compared with the regularity of
\rho\mathbf{u}\in C\left([0,T];L^{2}\left(\mathbb{T}^{3}\right)\right)
in
[
6
]
,
\rho\mathbf{u}
exhibits lower regularity in spatial variables in this paper.

2.

Low regularity of
\mathbf{u}
on account of degenerate viscosities
. Compared with the case of constant viscosity coefficients in
[
6
]
, the equation
(\ref{sto NS system})_{2}
becomes degenerate since the diffusion term
\operatorname{div}(\rho\mathbb{D}\mathbf{u})
will vanish at vacuum. We cannot obtain the estimate of
\left\|\mathbb{D}\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{2}([0,T]\times\left(\mathbb{T}^{3}\right))}
from the term
\operatorname{div}(\rho\mathbb{D}\mathbf{u})
since
\rho
does not have positive lower bound. The lack of regularity of
\mathbf{u}
is an obstacle to the convergence of approximations for the nonlinear term
\rho\mathbf{u}\otimes\mathbf{u}
. To make up the lack of regularity of
\mathbf{u}
, motivated by
[
7
]
, we construct an approximated scheme by adding artificial damping terms
-r_{1}\rho|\mathbf{u}|^{2}\mathbf{u}-r_{2}\mathbf{u}
to the momentum equation.
-r_{1}\rho|\mathbf{u}|^{2}\mathbf{u}-r_{2}\mathbf{u}
are drag forces in shallow water model, where
r_{1}\rho|\mathbf{u}|^{2}\mathbf{u}
is in the turbulent regime, and
r_{2}\mathbf{u}
(
r_{2}\geqslant 0
) is for the laminar case. We add the term
\varepsilon\triangle^{2}\mathbf{u}
for the estimate of
\triangle\mathbf{u}
, see
[
41
]
. Inspired by
[
39
]
, we added the quantum term
\kappa\rho\left(\nabla\left(\frac{\triangle\sqrt{\rho}}{\sqrt{\rho}}\right)\right)
, the artificial pressure term
\delta\rho\nabla\triangle^{9}\rho+\eta\rho^{-10}
to provide more regularity for
\rho
and
\mathbf{u}
. For the theory about quantum Navier-Stokes equations, please refer to
[
13
,
26
,
18
,
24
]
. However, for our stochastic case, since
\rho\mathbf{u}\in C\left([0,T];L^{\frac{3}{2}}\left(\mathbb{T}^{3}\right)\right)
, these regularities are still not enough for the convergence of approximations of the nonlinear term
\rho\mathbf{u}\otimes\mathbf{u}
, and we find that the desired regularity of
\mathbf{u}
can be obtained by adding an artificial damping term
-r_{0}|\mathbf{u}|^{2}\mathbf{u}
which is exactly Rayleigh damping term
[
14
,
33
]
. When we estimate the stochastic integral, the high regularity of
\rho
from the stochastic B-D entropy estimates makes up the lack of regularity of
\mathbf{u}
.

3.

Order of passing to the limit in stochastic case.
When vanishing these artificial terms to recover the original system, we have to take the order of passing to the limit different from the deterministic case. For example, Vasseur-Yu
[
38
]
vanished artificial pressure terms before vanishing quantum terms. For the stochastic case, if taking the same order, we would not have the uniform bound of the Rayleigh damping term
-r_{0}|\mathbf{u}|^{2}\mathbf{u}
in the stochastic B-D entropy. Hence we vanish the quantum term
\kappa\rho\left(\nabla\left(\frac{\triangle\sqrt{\rho}}{\sqrt{\rho}}\right)\right)
first, then let the Rayleigh damping term
-r_{0}|\mathbf{u}|^{2}\mathbf{u}
vanish at the same time with the artificial pressure terms
\delta\rho\nabla\triangle^{9}\rho
and
\eta\rho^{-10}
. In addition, we use Itô’s formula instead of the renormalization of momentum equation to deduce the stochastic Mellet-Vasseur type inequality. Vasseur-Yu
[
38
]
renormalized the momentum equation to obtain the convergence of terms for the Mellet-Vasseur’s inequality. Without the “renormalized” solutions for our stochastic case, the arising trouble of convergence is dealt by the regularity of artificial pressure terms and the subtle order of passing limit.

The paper is organized as follows. In §2, we first show the global-in-time existence of solutions to stochastic quantum Navier-Stokes system with the damping terms and the artificial pressures. In §3, we establish the stochastic B-D entropy estimates uniformly in
\varepsilon
, then we take the limit
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
. In §4, we derive the Mellet-Vasseur inequality in stochastic version, meanwhile let
\delta\rightarrow 0
,
\eta\rightarrow 0
,
r_{0}\rightarrow 0
,
n\rightarrow\infty
. Finally we prove Theorem
1.1
by sending the limit
r_{1}\rightarrow 0
and
r_{2}\rightarrow 0
. §5 is Appendix, the basic theories in Stochastic analysis is listed for the convenience of readers.

2.
Global existence of martingale solutions to the regularized system

In this section, we first construct a parabolic approximation structure to
\eqref{sto quantum damping NS system}_{1}
, and then apply Galerkin approximation to
\eqref{sto quantum damping NS system}_{1}
. Then like
[
4
,
27
]
, for the convenience of energy estimates, we truncate
|\mathbf{u}|
by
R
, dividing the time interval
[0,T]
into
\frac{T}{h}
parts, next we construct an iteration scheme (
2.8
). Next, we pass to the limit
h\rightarrow 0
, which exactly shows that the limit of solution of (
2.8
) satisfies the system (
2.1
) for
\forall t
. Then we let
R\rightarrow+\infty
to achieve the global existence of the Galerkin approximated regularized system. At the end of this section, we aim to obtain the global existence of martingale solutions in infinite dimensional space by sending
m\rightarrow+\infty
.

2.1.
Galerkin approximation of the regularized system in finite dimensional space
H_{m}

Let
\{w_{\mathbf{k}}\}_{|\mathbf{k}|=1}^{+\infty}
be an orthogonal basis of
L^{2}(\mathbb{T}^{3})
,
\mathbf{k}=(k_{1},k_{2},k_{3})
,
|\mathbf{k}|=\max\limits_{j=1,2,3}k_{j}
, more precisely,
w_{\mathbf{k}}(\mathbf{x})=e^{i2\pi\mathbf{k}\cdot\mathbf{x}}
.
The finite-dimensional space is defined as

(2.1)

H_{m}=\left\{w=\sum\limits_{\mathbf{m},\max|m_{i}|\leqslant m}a_{\mathbf{m}}\cos 2\pi\mathbf{m}\cdot\mathbf{x}+b_{\mathbf{m}}\sin 2\pi\mathbf{m}\cdot\mathbf{x}\right\}^{3}.

For any
f\in W^{k,p}(\mathbb{T}^{3})
,
\{w_{\mathbf{k}}\}_{|\mathbf{k}|=1}^{+\infty}
is also an orthogonal basis of
W^{k,p}(\mathbb{T}^{3})
,
\Pi_{m}\left(f\right)=\sum\limits_{\left|\mathbf{k}\right|=1}^{m}\langle f,w_{\mathbf{k}}\rangle w_{\mathbf{k}}
. By choosing appropriate
a_{\mathbf{m}}
and
b_{\mathbf{m}}
(cf.
[
19
]
), it holds
\Pi_{m}\left(f\right)\rightarrow f
in
W^{k,p}\left(\mathbb{T}^{3}\right)
as
m\rightarrow\infty
,
1\leqslant p<+\infty
.

We employ the following Galerkin approximation scheme for (
1.20
):

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\vskip 5.16663pt\rho_{t}+\operatorname{div}(\rho\mathbf{u})=\varepsilon\triangle\rho,\\
\operatorname{d}\Pi_{m}\left(\rho\mathbf{u}\right)+\Pi_{m}\left(\operatorname{div}(\rho\mathbf{u}\otimes\mathbf{u})+\nabla\left(a\rho^{\gamma}\right)-\operatorname{div}(\rho\mathbb{D}\mathbf{u})-\frac{11}{10}\eta\nabla\rho^{-10}\right)\operatorname{d}t\\
=\Pi_{m}\left(\left(-r_{0}|\mathbf{u}|^{2}\mathbf{u}-r_{1}\rho|\mathbf{u}|^{2}\mathbf{u}-r_{2}\mathbf{u}\right)\right)\operatorname{d}t-\Pi_{m}\left(\varepsilon\nabla\rho\nabla\mathbf{u}\right)\operatorname{d}t-\Pi_{m}\left(\varepsilon\triangle^{2}\mathbf{u}\right)\operatorname{d}t\\
+\Pi_{m}\left(\delta\rho\nabla\triangle^{9}\rho\right)\operatorname{d}t+\kappa\Pi_{m}\left(\rho\left(\nabla\left(\frac{\triangle\sqrt{\rho}}{\sqrt{\rho}}\right)\right)\right)\operatorname{d}t+\Pi_{m}\left(\rho\mathbb{F}(\rho,\mathbf{u})\right)\operatorname{d}W.\\
\end{array}\right.

By the definition of
\Pi_{m}
,
\rho_{0}
is approximated by
\Pi_{m}\left(\rho_{0}\right)
, by choosing suitable coefficients
a_{\mathbf{m}},b_{\mathbf{m}}
, there exists a positive constant
\underline{\rho}
and
\Pi_{m_{k}}\left(\rho_{0}\right)
, a subsequence of
\Pi_{m}\left(\rho_{0}\right)
,
\Pi_{m_{k}}\left(\rho_{0}\right)\geqslant\underline{\rho}
, where
\underline{\rho}
changes with
m_{k}
. The subsequence is still denoted by
\Pi_{m}\left(\rho_{0}\right)
for convenience.
By the maximum principle of the parabolic structure and
\eqref{Galerkin approximation scheme}_{1}
, we have

(2.6)

\inf\limits_{x\in\mathbb{T}^{3}}\Pi_{m}\left(\rho_{0}\right)e^{-\int_{0}^{t}\left\|\operatorname{div}\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\operatorname{d}s}\leqslant\rho\leqslant\sup\limits_{x\in\mathbb{T}^{3}}\Pi_{m}\left(\rho_{0}\right)e^{-\int_{0}^{t}\left\|\operatorname{div}\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\operatorname{d}s}.

In the finite dimensional space
H_{m}
, it naturally holds
\Lambda\left(\{\Pi_{m}\left(\rho_{0}\right)\leqslant\bar{\rho}\}\right)=1
, where
\bar{\rho}
is a constant;
\Lambda\left(\left\{\left\|\Pi_{m}\left(\mathbf{q}_{0}\right)\right\|_{H_{m}}\leqslant\bar{q}\right\}\right)=1
, where
\bar{q}
is a positive constant, and
\underline{\rho}
,
\bar{q}
and
\bar{\rho}
may change with
m
.
For the sake of (
2.6
), we define a cut-off function in
C^{\infty}\left(\mathds{R}^{3}\right)
:

(2.7)

\chi_{R}(z)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}1,\quad\text{for}\quad\left\|z\right\|_{H_{m}}\leqslant R,\\
0\leqslant\chi_{R}(z)\leqslant 1,\quad\text{for}\quad R\leqslant\left\|z\right\|_{H_{m}}\leqslant R+1,\\
0,\quad\text{for}\quad\left\|z\right\|_{H_{m}}\geqslant R+1.\\
\end{array}\right.

[\mathbf{u}]_{R}=\chi_{R}(\mathbf{u})\mathbf{u}
represents the truncation of
\mathbf{u}
.

2.2.
The global existence of martingale solutions of the regularized approximated system in
H_{m}

We get the solutions of (
2.1
) by linearization, iteration, uniform energy estimates and stochastic tightness theory.

2.2.1.
Linearization

We split the time interval
[0,T]
into grid, setting
h
as the step length. We define the approximated iteration scheme recursively,

(2.8)

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} \vskip 5.16663pt&\rho_{t}+\operatorname{div}\left(\rho(t)[\mathbf{u}(nh)]_{R}\right)=\varepsilon\triangle\rho(t),\quad t\in[nh,(n+1)h],\\
&\operatorname{d}\Pi_{m}\left(\rho\mathbf{u}\right)+\Pi_{m}\left(\operatorname{div}(\rho(t)\left[\mathbf{u}(nh)\right]_{R}\otimes\mathbf{u}(nh))\right)\operatorname{d}t+\Pi_{m}\left(\chi_{R}(\mathbf{u}(nh))\nabla p(\rho(t))\right)\operatorname{d}t\\
&-\left(\Pi_{m}\left(\chi_{R}(\mathbf{u}(nh))\operatorname{div}\left(\rho\mathbb{D}\mathbf{u}(nh)\right)\right)\right)\operatorname{d}t\\
&+\left(\Pi_{m}\left(\frac{11}{10}\eta\nabla\rho^{-10}\chi_{R}\left(\mathbf{u}(nh)\right)+\chi_{R}(\mathbf{u}(nh))\delta\rho\nabla\triangle^{9}\rho\right)\right)\operatorname{d}t\\
&=\Pi_{m}\left((-r_{0}|\mathbf{u}(nh)|^{2}\left[\mathbf{u}(nh)\right]_{R}-r_{1}\rho|\mathbf{u}(nh)|^{2}\left[\mathbf{u}(nh)\right]_{R}-r_{2}\left[\mathbf{u}(nh)\right]_{R})\right)\operatorname{d}t\\
&-\varepsilon\Pi_{m}\left(\chi_{R}(\mathbf{u}(nh))\nabla\rho\nabla\mathbf{u}(nh)\right)\operatorname{d}t-\varepsilon\Pi_{m}\left(\chi_{R}(\mathbf{u}(nh))\triangle^{2}\mathbf{u}(nh)\right)\operatorname{d}t\\
&+\kappa\Pi_{m}\left(\chi_{R}(\mathbf{u}(nh))\rho\left(\nabla\left(\frac{\triangle\sqrt{\rho}}{\sqrt{\rho}}\right)\right)\right)\operatorname{d}t+\Pi_{m}\left(\chi_{R}(\mathbf{u}(nh))\rho\Pi_{m}\left[\mathbb{F}(\rho,\mathbf{u}(nh))\right]\right)\operatorname{d}W,\\
&\rho(nh)=\rho(nh-)=\lim\limits_{s\rightarrow nh}\rho(s),\quad\rho(0)=\Pi_{m}\left(\rho_{0}\right),\\
&\mathbf{u}(nh)=\mathbf{u}(nh-)=\lim\limits_{s\rightarrow nh}\mathbf{u}(s),\quad\rho\mathbf{u}(0)=\mathbf{q}_{0}.\end{aligned}\right.

We introduce a linear mapping
\mathscr{M}\left(\rho\right)
,

(2.9)

\mathscr{M}\left(\rho\right):H_{m}\rightarrow H_{m}^{*},\quad\mathscr{M}\left(\rho\right)(\mathbf{z})=\Pi_{m}(\rho\mathbf{z}),

or

(2.10)

\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\mathscr{M}\left(\rho\right)\mathbf{z}\cdot\mathbf{\varphi}_{m}\operatorname{d}x\equiv\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\Pi_{m}(\rho\mathbf{z})\cdot\mathbf{\varphi}_{m}\operatorname{d}x\quad\text{ for all }\mathbf{\varphi}_{m}\in H_{m}.

From (
2.8
),
\mathscr{M}\left(\rho\right)
is invertible for
\rho\neq 0
, we write
\rho=S(\mathbf{u}(nh),\Pi_{m}\left(\rho_{0}\right))
,
S
is a Lipschitz-continuous operator. This gives a representation of
\mathbf{u}
explicitly. For any
h
, the stochastic equation admits a unique solution in
[nh,(n+1)h]
, since the stochastic equation admits a unique solution in a time interval
[0,t)
for any
t
.
\left\|\mathbf{u}\right\|_{H_{m}}\leqslant C
is necessary for taking the limit
h\rightarrow 0
, where
C
is independent of
R
,
h
, time
\tau
,
\tau\in[0,T]
, which will be showed in the following.
The solution of (
2.8
) are concerned with
h,R,m,\varepsilon,\kappa,\eta,\delta,r_{0},r_{1}
, and
r_{2}
, since the limit
h\rightarrow 0
is mainly focused in this subsection, and for simplicity, we denote the solutions as
\rho_{m,h}
and
\mathbf{u}_{m,h}
. During the limit taking process, we will use the following notations:

(2.11)

\displaystyle\rho_{m,h}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle h\rightarrow 0}}{{\longrightarrow}}\rho_{m,R}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle R\rightarrow\infty}}{{\longrightarrow}}\rho_{m}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle m\rightarrow\infty}}{{\longrightarrow}}\rho_{\rm reg}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}}{{\longrightarrow}}\rho_{{\rm I}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\kappa\rightarrow 0}}{{\longrightarrow}}\rho_{{\rm II}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eta,\delta,r_{0}\rightarrow 0}}{{\longrightarrow}}\rho_{{\rm III}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle r_{1},r_{2}\rightarrow 0}}{{\longrightarrow}}\rho,

(2.12)

\displaystyle\mathbf{u}_{m,h}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle h\rightarrow 0}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathbf{u}_{m,R}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle R\rightarrow\infty}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathbf{u}_{m}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle m\rightarrow\infty}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathbf{u}_{\rm reg}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\varepsilon\rightarrow 0}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathbf{u}_{{\rm I}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\kappa\rightarrow 0}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathbf{u}_{{\rm II}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\eta,\delta,r_{0}\rightarrow 0}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathbf{u}_{{\rm III}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle r_{1},r_{2}\rightarrow 0}}{{\longrightarrow}}\mathbf{u},

where
\rho_{m,R}
and
\mathbf{u}_{m,R}
are concerned with
R,m,\varepsilon,\kappa,\eta,\delta,r_{0},r_{1}
, and
r_{2}
;
\rho_{m}
and
\mathbf{u}_{m}
are concerned with
m,\varepsilon,\delta,\eta,r_{0},r_{1},r_{2}
and
\kappa
;
\rho_{\rm reg}
and
\mathbf{u}_{\rm reg}
are concerned with
\varepsilon,\kappa,\eta,\delta,r_{0},r_{1}
, and
r_{2}
;
\rho_{{\rm I}}
and
\mathbf{u}_{{\rm I}}
are concerned with
\kappa,\eta,\delta,r_{0},r_{1}
, and
r_{2}
;
\rho_{{\rm II}}
and
\mathbf{u}_{{\rm II}}
are concerned with
\eta,\delta,r_{0},r_{1}
, and
r_{2}
;
\rho_{{\rm III}}
and
\mathbf{u}_{{\rm III}}
are concerned with
r_{1}
and
r_{2}
;
\rho
and
\mathbf{u}
are solutions to the original system.

From (
2.8
), by Burkhölder-Davis-Gundy’s inequality (see Appendix
5.2
) and the fact that norms are equivalent in
H_{m}
,
the following estimate holds uniformly in
h
and
\tau
:

(2.13)

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}[\left\|\rho_{m,h}\mathbf{u}_{m,h}(\tau)\right\|_{H_{m}}^{r}]+\mathbb{E}[\left\|\mathbf{u}_{m,h}(\tau)\right\|_{H_{m}}^{r}]\leqslant C\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\mathbf{q}_{0}\right\|_{H_{m}}^{r}\right]+1\right),\\
\end{split}

where
C
depends on
m,R,T,\underline{\rho}(m),\bar{\rho}
and
r

[
4
]
. Moreover, it holds
\rho_{m,h}|_{t=0}=\rho_{0}
,
\rho_{m,h}\mathbf{u}_{m,h}|_{t=0}=\mathbf{q}_{0}
,
\mathbb{P}
a.s.

To determine the space of
\rho_{m,h}
and
\mathbf{u}_{m,h}
, for (
2.1
), we estimate

(2.14)

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}\left[\Pi_{m}\left\|\rho_{m,h}\mathbf{u}_{m,h}(t_{1},\cdot)-\rho_{m,h}\mathbf{u}_{m,h}(t_{2},\cdot)\right\|^{r}_{H_{m}}\right]\leqslant C(t_{1}-t_{2})^{\frac{r}{2}}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\mathbf{q}_{0}\right\|_{H_{m}}^{r}\right]+1\right),\\
\end{split}

for
r>1
,
0\leqslant t_{1},t_{2}\leqslant T
, since the deterministic nonlinearity terms are linearized and
\mathbf{u}_{m,h}
is truncated by
R
. Since
\rho_{m,h}(t)
is lower bounded by
\underline{\rho}(m)
, upper bounded by
\bar{\rho}
, by Kolmogorov-Centov’s continuity theorem, it holds

(2.15)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\tilde{\mathbf{u}}_{m,h}\right\|^{r}_{{C_{t}^{0,\beta}}H_{m}}\right]\leqslant C\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\mathbf{q}_{0}\right\|_{H_{m}}^{r}\right]+1\right),

where
\tilde{\mathbf{u}}_{m,h}
is a modification of
\mathbf{u}_{m,h}
, and we still use the notation
\mathbf{u}_{m,h}
in the following,
r>2
,
\beta\in\left(0,\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{r}\right)
and
C
only depends on
m,R,\underline{\rho}(m),\bar{\rho},T
and
r
.
For
\rho_{m,h}
, (
2.8
) implies

\displaystyle\left\|\rho_{m,h}(\tau_{1})-\rho_{m,h}(\tau_{2})\right\|_{H_{m}}\leqslant\left\|\int_{\tau_{1}}^{\tau_{2}}\operatorname{div}\left(\rho_{m,h}[\mathbf{u}_{m,h}(nh)]_{R}\right)\operatorname{d}t\right\|_{H_{m}}+\left\|\int_{\tau_{2}}^{\tau_{1}}\varepsilon\triangle\rho_{m,h}\operatorname{d}t\right\|_{H_{m}}

(2.16)

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle C\left\|\rho_{m,h}\right\|_{H_{m}}\left(\tau_{1}-\tau_{2}\right),

where
C
depends on
R,m
, so
\rho_{m,h}\in C^{0,1}\left([0,T];H_{m}\right)
, the sequence
\{\left\|\rho_{m,h}\right\|_{H_{m}}\}_{h}
is equiv-continuous in
C([0,T])
.

We define the solution space
\mathcal{X}_{1}=\mathcal{X}_{\rho}^{1}\times\mathcal{X}_{\mathbf{u}}^{1}\times\mathcal{X}_{W}^{1}=C\left([0,T];H_{m}\right)

\times C^{0,\nu}\left([0,T];H_{m}\right)\times C\left([0,T];\mathcal{H}\right)
,
0<\nu<\beta
,
\beta\in\left(0,\frac{1}{2}-\frac{1}{r}\right)
, for some
r>2
.
We consider the set

(2.17)

B_{\rho,m}=\left\{\left.\rho_{m,h}\in C^{0,1}\left([0,T];H_{m}\right)\right|\|\rho_{m,h}\|_{C^{0,1}\left([0,T];H_{m}\right)}\leq L\right\},

which is relatively compact in
\mathcal{X}_{\rho}^{1}
due to Arzelà-Ascoli’s theorem. In fact, by Arzelà-Ascoli’s continuity theorem, we gain that
\{\left\|\rho_{m,h}\right\|_{H_{m}}\}_{h}
is precompact in a subset of
C\left([0,T]\right)
, i.e.,
C^{0,1}\left([0,T];H_{m}\right)

\hookrightarrow C\left([0,T];H_{m}\right)
.
Due to Chebyshev’s inequality, we get

(2.18)

\mathcal{L}\left[\rho_{m,h}\right]\left(B_{\rho,m}^{c}\right)=\mathbb{P}\left[\left\{\left\|\rho_{m,h}\right\|_{C_{t}^{0,1}H_{m}}>L\right\}\right]\leq\frac{1}{L^{r}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\rho_{m,h}\right\|_{C_{t}^{0,1}H_{m}}^{r}\right]\leq\frac{C}{L^{r}},

where
C
is uniformly in
h
. Choosing
L
sufficiently large yields the tightness of
\mathcal{L}\left[\rho_{m,h}\right]
. Similarly,

(2.19)

B_{\mathbf{u},m}=\left\{\left.\mathbf{u}_{m,h}\in C^{0,\beta}\left([0,T];H_{m}\right)\right|\|\mathbf{u}_{m,h}\|_{C_{t}^{0,\beta}H_{m}}\leq L\right\},

by Chebyshev’s inequality,

(2.20)

\mathcal{L}\left[\mathbf{u}_{m,h}\right]\left(B_{\mathbf{u},m}^{c}\right)=\mathbb{P}\left[\left\{\left\|\mathbf{u}_{m,h}\right\|_{C_{t}^{0,\beta}H_{m}}>L\right\}\right]\leq\frac{1}{L^{r}}\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\mathbf{u}_{m,h}\right\|_{C_{t}^{0,\beta}H_{m}}^{r}\right]\leq\frac{C}{L^{r}},

which shows the tightness of
\mathcal{L}\left[\mathbf{u}_{m,h}\right]
.

\mathcal{L}[W]
is tight on
\mathcal{X}_{W}
since it is a Radon measure on a Polish space.
To conclude, for any given small
\iota>0
, there exist compact sets
K_{\varrho}\subset\mathcal{X}_{\varrho}^{1},K_{\mathbf{u}}\subset

\mathcal{X}_{\mathbf{u}}^{1}
,
K_{W}\subset\mathcal{X}_{W}^{1}
such that

(2.21)

\mathcal{L}\left[\rho_{m,h}\right]\left(K_{\rho}\right)\geq 1-\frac{\iota}{3},\quad\mathcal{L}\left[\mathbf{u}_{m,h}\right]\left(K_{\mathbf{u}}\right)\geq 1-\frac{\iota}{3},\quad\mathcal{L}[W]\left(K_{W}\right)\geq 1-\frac{\iota}{3}.

Applying de Morgan’s law
A\cap B=\left(A^{c}\cup B^{c}\right)^{c}
, we get

(2.22)

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}\left[\rho_{m,h},\mathbf{u}_{m,h},W\right]\left(K_{\rho}\times K_{\mathbf{u}}\times K_{W}\right)=\mathbb{P}\left[\left\{\rho_{m,h}\in K_{\rho},\mathbf{u}_{m,h}\in K_{\mathbf{u}},W\in K_{W}\right\}\right]

\displaystyle=1-\mathbb{P}\left[\left\{\rho_{m,h}\notin K_{\rho}\right\}\cup\left\{\mathbf{u}_{m,h}\notin K_{\mathbf{u}}\right\}\cup\left\{W\notin K_{W}\right\}\right]

\displaystyle\geq 1-\mathbb{P}\left[\left\{\rho_{m,h}\notin K_{\rho}\right\}\right]-\mathbb{P}\left[\left\{\mathbf{u}_{m,h}\notin K_{\mathbf{u}}\right\}\right]-\mathbb{P}\left[\left\{W\notin K_{W}\right\}\right]\geq 1-\iota,

this gives the tightness of
\mathcal{L}[\rho_{m,h},\mathbf{u}_{m,h},W]
.

Based on the tightness of
\mathcal{L}[\rho_{m,h},\mathbf{u}_{m,h},W]
and Jakubowski-Skorokhod’s representation theorem (see
2.
in Appendix
5.2
), we have the following convergence theorem.

Proposition 2.1
.

There exist
\left\{\bar{\rho}_{m,h},\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m,h},\bar{W}_{m,h}\right\}
and
\left\{\rho_{m,R},\mathbf{u}_{m,R},W_{m,R}\right\}
, two families of
\mathcal{X}_{1}
-valued Borel measurable random variables, defined on a complete probability space
\left(\bar{\Omega},\bar{\mathcal{F}},\mathbb{\bar{P}}\right)
, such that

(1)

\mathcal{L}\left[\bar{\rho}_{m,h},\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m,h},\bar{W}_{m,h}\right]
on
\mathcal{X}_{1}
is given by
\mathcal{L}\left[\rho_{m,h},\mathbf{u}_{m,h},W\right]
;

(2)

\mathcal{L}\left[\rho_{m,R},\mathbf{u}_{m,R},W_{m,R}\right]
on
\mathcal{X}_{1}
is a Radon measure;

(3)

\left\{\bar{\rho}_{m,h},\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m,h},\bar{W}_{m,h}\right\}
converges to
\left\{\rho_{m,R},\mathbf{u}_{m,R},W_{m,R}\right\}

\bar{\mathbb{P}}

a.s.
in the topology of
\mathcal{X}_{1}
, i.e.,

(2.23)

\displaystyle\bar{\rho}_{m,h}\rightarrow\rho_{m,R}

\displaystyle\text{ in }C\left([0,T];H_{m,R}\right)\quad\bar{\mathbb{P}}\text{ {\rm a.s.}};

\displaystyle\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m,h}\rightarrow\mathbf{u}_{m,R}

\displaystyle\text{ in }C^{0,\nu}\left([0,T];H_{m,R}\right)\quad\bar{\mathbb{P}}\text{ {\rm a.s.}};

\displaystyle\bar{W}_{m,h}\rightarrow W_{m,R}

\displaystyle\text{ in }C\left([0,T];\mathcal{H}\right)\quad\bar{\mathbb{P}}\text{ {\rm a.s.}}

Here we omit the proof which can be obtained by using similar arguments as in
[
4
]
.

Proposition 2.2
.

The processes
\rho_{m,R}
and
\mathbf{u}_{m,R}
satisfy

(2.24)

\Pi_{m}\left(\left(\rho_{m,R}\right)_{t}\right)+\Pi_{m}\left(\operatorname{div}\left(\rho_{m,R}\left[\mathbf{u}_{m,R}\right]_{R}\right)\right)=\Pi_{m}\left(\varepsilon\triangle\rho_{m,R}\right)\\

in distribution and
\bar{\mathbb{P}}

a.s.

Proof.

Firstly, the new processes hold
\mathcal{L}[\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m,h}]=\mathcal{L}[\mathbf{u}_{m,h}],\mathcal{L}[\bar{\rho}_{m,h}]=\mathcal{L}[\rho_{m,h}]
, hence
\bar{\rho}_{m,h}
and
\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{mh}
still satisfy the continuity equation
\bar{\mathbb{P}}
a.s., namely,

(2.25)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\Pi_{m}\left(\left(\bar{\rho}_{m,h}\right)_{t}+\operatorname{div}\left(\bar{\rho}_{m,h}\left[\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m,h}\right]_{R}\right)\right)\varphi(t)\psi(x)\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}s

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\Pi_{m}\left(\varepsilon\triangle\bar{\rho}_{m,h}\right)\varphi(t)\psi(x)\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}s,\quad t\in[0,T],

for all
\varphi\in C_{c}^{\infty}\left([0,T)\right)
, and all
\psi(x)\in C^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{T}^{3}\right)
,
\bar{\mathbb{P}}
a.s. For
\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\Pi_{m}\left(\bar{\rho}_{m,h}(t)\right)\varphi(t)\psi(x)\mathrm{d}x
,
\bar{\rho}_{m,h}\rightarrow\rho_{m,R}\text{ in }C\left([0,T];H_{m}\right)
,
\bar{\mathbb{P}}
a.s., therefore
\Pi_{m}\left(\bar{\rho}_{m,h}(t,x)\right)\varphi(t)\psi(x)
is uniformly integrable in
L^{1}(\Omega\times[0,T]\times\mathbb{T}^{3})
, by Vitali’s convergence theorem, we have

(2.26)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\Pi_{m}\left(\bar{\rho}_{m,h}(t,x)\right)\varphi(t)\psi(x)\mathrm{d}x\rightarrow\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\Pi_{m}\left(\rho_{m,R}(t,x)\right)\varphi(t)\psi(x)\mathrm{d}x\quad\bar{\mathbb{P}}\text{ a.s.}

Similarly, we have the convergence of other integrals in (
2.25
). So we have

\displaystyle\varphi(t)\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\Pi_{m}\left(\rho_{m,R}(t,x)\right)\psi(x)\mathrm{d}x-\varphi(0)\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\Pi_{m}\left[\rho_{m,R}(0,x)\right]\psi(x)\mathrm{d}x

(2.27)

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{t}\varphi_{s}(s)\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\Pi_{m}\left(\rho_{m,R}\right)(s,x)\psi(x)\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}s

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\varphi(s)\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\Pi_{m}\left[\operatorname{div}(\rho_{m,R}\left[\mathbf{u}_{m,R}\right]_{R})\right]\psi(x)\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}s

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\varphi(s)\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\Pi_{m}\left(\varepsilon\triangle\rho_{m,R}\right)\psi(x)\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}s,

for all
\varphi\in C_{c}^{\infty}\left([0,T)\right)
, and all
\psi(x)\in C^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{T}^{3}\right)
, and
t\in[0,T),\quad\bar{\mathbb{P}}
a.s.
\square

For simplicity, we give the new notations here:

(2.28)

\displaystyle\left\|\cdot\right\|_{L^{p}(0,T;L^{q}\left(\mathbb{T}^{3}\right))}=\left\|\cdot\right\|_{L_{t}^{p}L_{x}^{q}},\text{ for }1\leqslant p\leqslant\infty,1\leqslant q\leqslant\infty,

\displaystyle\left\|\cdot\right\|_{L^{p}(0,T;H^{q}\left(\mathbb{T}^{3}\right))}=\left\|\cdot\right\|_{L_{t}^{p}H_{x}^{q}},\text{ for }1\leqslant p\leqslant\infty,1\leqslant q<\infty.

To show that the limits
\rho_{m}
and
\mathbf{u}_{m}
satisfy the approximated momentum equation, we first generalize the convergence theorem of stochastic integral
[
12
]
from “in probability” to “
\bar{\mathbb{P}}
a.s.” as follows.

Lemma 2.1
.

If
\mathbf{G}_{k}\rightarrow\mathbf{G}
in
L^{2}\left([0,T]\times\mathbb{T}^{3}\right)

\mathbb{P}

a.s.
,
W_{k}\rightarrow W
in
C\left([0,T];\mathcal{H}\right)

\mathbb{P}

a.s.
, then

(2.29)

\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\mathbf{G}_{k}\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}W_{k}\rightarrow\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\mathbf{G}\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}W\quad\mathbb{P}\text{ {\rm a.s.}}\\

Proof.

Since
W_{k}\rightarrow W
as
k\rightarrow\infty
, so there exists a constant
k_{1}=\max\{1,K_{1}\}>0
, such that for
k>k_{1}
,
\left|W_{k}-W\right|\leqslant 1
. And there exists a constant
k_{2}=\max\{2,K_{2}\}>k_{1}
, such that for
k>k_{2}
,
\left|W_{k}-W\right|\leqslant\frac{1}{k_{1}^{2}}
. All the time all the way, there exists
k_{n+1}=\max\{n+1,K_{n+1}\}>k_{n}
, when
k>k_{n+1}
, it holds
|W_{k}-W|<\frac{1}{k_{n}^{2}}
.

\displaystyle\left|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\mathbf{G}_{k}\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}W_{k}-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\mathbf{G}\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}W\right|

(2.30)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\mathbf{G}_{k}\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}W_{k}-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\mathbf{G}\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}W_{k}+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\mathbf{G}\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}W_{k}-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\mathbf{G}\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}W\right|

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle\left|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\left(\mathbf{G}_{k}-\mathbf{G}\right)\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}W_{k}\right|+\left|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\mathbf{G}\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}W_{k}-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\mathbf{G}\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}W\right|.

The first term in the right hand side of the above formula goes to
0
,
\mathbb{P}
a.s., as
k\rightarrow+\infty
. Actually, for any
\varepsilon>0
, for
k
large enough,
\left|\mathbf{G}_{k}-\mathbf{G}\right|\leqslant\varepsilon^{2}
,

(2.31)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\left|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\left(\mathbf{G}_{k}-\mathbf{G}\right)\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}W_{k}\right|\geq\varepsilon\right]

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\left(\mathbf{G}_{k}-\mathbf{G}\right)\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}W_{k}\right|^{2}\right]}{\varepsilon^{2}}\leqslant\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{t}\left|\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\left(\mathbf{G}_{k}-\mathbf{G}\right)\operatorname{d}x\right|^{2}\operatorname{d}t\right]}{\varepsilon^{2}}\leqslant\varepsilon^{2}.

Now we focus on the second term. We define a mollification
\bar{\mathbf{G}}=\frac{1}{\iota}\int_{0}^{t}\exp^{-\frac{t-s}{\iota}}\mathbf{G}(s)\operatorname{d}s
, here
\iota\in\left\{1,\frac{1}{k_{1}},\frac{1}{k_{2}},\cdots,\frac{1}{k_{n}},\cdots\right\}
. By Heine’s theorem,
\bar{\mathbf{G}}\rightarrow\mathbf{G}
still holds as
\iota\rightarrow 0
. We estimate

\displaystyle\left|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\mathbf{G}\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}W_{k}-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\mathbf{G}\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}W\right|

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle\left|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\left(\mathbf{G}-\bar{\mathbf{G}}\right)\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}W_{k}+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\left(\bar{\mathbf{G}}-\mathbf{G}\right)\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}W\right.

(2.32)

\displaystyle\left.+\left(\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\bar{\mathbf{G}}\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}W_{k}-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\bar{\mathbf{G}}\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}W\right)\right|

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle\left|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\left(\mathbf{G}-\bar{\mathbf{G}}\right)\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}W_{k}\right|+\left|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\left(\bar{\mathbf{G}}-\mathbf{G}\right)\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}W\right|

\displaystyle+\left|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\bar{\mathbf{G}}\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}W_{k}-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\bar{\mathbf{G}}\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}W\right|.

Like (
2.31
), the first term and the second term in the right-hand-side of (
2.2.1
) will converge to
0
as
\iota\rightarrow 0
.
For the term
\left|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\bar{\mathbf{G}}\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}W_{k}-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\bar{\mathbf{G}}\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}W\right|
, we calculate

(2.33)

\frac{\operatorname{d}\bar{\mathbf{G}}}{\operatorname{d}t}=\frac{1}{\iota}\mathbf{G}(t)-\frac{1}{\iota^{2}}\int_{0}^{t}\exp^{-\frac{t-s}{\iota}}\mathbf{G}(s)\operatorname{d}s=\frac{1}{\iota}\mathbf{G}(t)-\frac{1}{\iota}\bar{\mathbf{G}}.\\

For the third integral in (
2.2.1
), we obtain

\displaystyle\left|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\bar{\mathbf{G}}\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}W_{k}-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\bar{\mathbf{G}}\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}W\right|

(2.34)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\bar{\mathbf{G}}\operatorname{d}xW_{k}-\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\bar{\mathbf{G}}\operatorname{d}xW-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\frac{\operatorname{d}\bar{\mathbf{G}}}{\operatorname{d}s}W_{k}\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\frac{\operatorname{d}\bar{\mathbf{G}}}{\operatorname{d}s}W\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}s\right|

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\bar{\mathbf{G}}\operatorname{d}x\left(W_{k}-W\right)\right|+\left|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\left(W-W_{k}\right)\frac{\mathbf{G}}{\iota}\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}s\right|

\displaystyle+\left|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\left(W-W_{k}\right)\frac{\bar{\mathbf{G}}}{\iota}\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}s\right|.

by Itô’s formula and the integration by parts with respect to
t
for the stochastic integral of semi-martingale (see §
5.1
in Appendix).

\left|\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\bar{\mathbf{G}}\operatorname{d}x\left(W_{k}-W\right)\right|
will converge to
0
. In fact, we calculate

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\bar{\mathbf{G}}\operatorname{d}x\left(W_{k}-W\right)\right|=\left|\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\sum\limits_{k=1}^{+\infty}\bar{\mathbf{G}}_{i}e_{i}\operatorname{d}x\sum\limits_{i=1}^{+\infty}\left(W_{k}-W\right)_{i}e_{i}\right|

(2.35)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left|\sum\limits_{i=1}^{+\infty}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\bar{\mathbf{G}}_{i}\operatorname{d}x\left(W_{k}-W\right)_{i}\right|\leqslant\sum\limits_{i=1}^{+\infty}\left|\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\bar{\mathbf{G}}_{i}\operatorname{d}x\right|\left|\left(W_{k}-W\right)_{i}\right|

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{i=1}^{+\infty}\left(\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\left|\bar{\mathbf{G}}_{i}\right|^{2}\operatorname{d}x\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left|\left(W_{k}-W\right)_{i}\right|\leqslant\|\bar{\mathbf{G}}\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}L_{x}^{2}}\sup_{i}\left|\left(W_{k}-W\right)_{i}\right|.

Since
W_{k}\rightarrow W
as
k\rightarrow\infty
, so for any
\iota>0
,
k>k_{n+1}
,

(2.36)

\displaystyle\left|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\left(W-W_{k}\right)\frac{\bar{\mathbf{G}}}{\iota}\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}s\right|\leqslant k_{n}\frac{1}{k_{n}^{2}}\left|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\bar{\mathbf{G}}\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}s\right|\rightarrow 0\text{ as }k_{n}\rightarrow+\infty.

So does the term
\left|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\left(W-W_{k}\right)\frac{\mathbf{G}}{\iota}\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}s\right|
.
\square

Now we have the following proposition for the approximated momentum equation.

Proposition 2.3
.

The processes
\left(\rho_{m,R},\mathbf{u}_{m,R},W_{m,R}\right)
satisfy

\displaystyle\operatorname{d}\Pi_{m}\left(\rho_{m,R}\mathbf{u}_{m,R}\right)+\Pi_{m}\left(\operatorname{div}(\rho_{m,R}[\mathbf{u}_{m,R}]_{R}\otimes\mathbf{u}_{m,R})+\nabla\left(a\rho_{m,R}^{\gamma}\right)\chi_{R}(\left\|\mathbf{u}_{m,R}\right\|_{H_{m}})\right)\operatorname{d}t

\displaystyle-\Pi_{m}\left(\operatorname{div}(\rho_{m,R}\mathbb{D}\mathbf{u}_{m,R})\chi_{R}(\left\|\mathbf{u}_{m,R}\right\|_{H_{m}})\right)\operatorname{d}t

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Pi_{m}\left((-r_{0}|\mathbf{u}_{m,R}|^{2}[\mathbf{u}_{m,R}]_{R}-r_{1}\rho_{m,R}|\mathbf{u}_{m,R}|^{2}[\mathbf{u}_{m,R}]_{R}-r_{2}[\mathbf{u}_{m,R}]_{R})\right)\operatorname{d}t

(2.37)

\displaystyle+\Pi_{m}\left(\varepsilon\nabla\rho_{m,R}\nabla\mathbf{u}_{m,R}\chi_{R}(\left\|\mathbf{u}_{m,R}\right\|_{H_{m}})\right)\operatorname{d}t-\Pi_{m}\left(\varepsilon\triangle^{2}\mathbf{u}_{m,R}\chi_{R}(\left\|\mathbf{u}_{m,R}\right\|_{H_{m}})\right)\operatorname{d}t

\displaystyle+\Pi_{m}\left(\frac{11}{10}\eta\nabla\rho_{m,R}^{-10}\chi_{R}(\left\|\mathbf{u}_{m,R}\right\|_{H_{m}})\right)\operatorname{d}t+\Pi_{m}\left(\delta\rho_{m,R}\nabla\triangle^{9}\rho_{m,R}\chi_{R}(\left\|\mathbf{u}_{m,R}\right\|_{H_{m}})\right)\operatorname{d}t

\displaystyle+\Pi_{m}\left(\kappa\rho_{m,R}\left(\nabla\left(\frac{\triangle\sqrt{\rho_{m,R}}}{\sqrt{\rho_{m,R}}}\right)\right)\chi_{R}\left(\left\|\mathbf{u}_{m,R}\right\|_{H_{m}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle+\Pi_{m}\left(\rho_{m,R}\mathbb{F}(\rho_{m,R},\mathbf{u}_{m,R})\chi_{R}\left(\left\|\mathbf{u}_{m,R}\right\|_{H_{m}}\right)\operatorname{d}W_{m,R}\right),

\bar{\mathbb{P}}

a.s.
in distribution sense, for
\forall t\in[0,T]
.

Proof.

We mainly need to show the convergence of stochastic term in this proof, because the other terms in momentum equation converges in distribution coming from the strong convergence of variables and Vitali’s convergence theorem. Similarly as in
[
4
]
, by the strong convergence of
\bar{\rho}_{m,h}
and
\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m,h}
, and the continuity of
\Pi_{m}\left(\mathbf{F}_{k}\right)
, it holds

(2.38)

\displaystyle\Pi_{m}\left(\bar{\rho}_{m,h}\Pi_{m}\left(\mathbf{F}_{k}\left(\bar{\rho}_{m,h},\bar{\mathbb{\mathbf{u}}}_{m,h}\right)\right)\right)\rightarrow\Pi_{m}\left(\rho_{m,R}\Pi_{m}\left(\mathbf{F}_{k}(\rho_{m,R},\mathbf{u}_{m,R})\right)\right),

\bar{\mathbb{P}}
a.s. in
L^{2}\left(\Omega;L^{2}\left([0,T];L^{2}\left(\mathbb{T}^{3}\right)\right)\right)
. Combining this with the convergence of
\bar{W}_{m,h}
, by lemma
2.1
, for all
\varphi(t)\in C_{c}^{\infty}\left([0,T)\right)
, and all
\psi(x)\in C^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{T}^{3}\right)
, we have

(2.39)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\Pi_{m}\left(\bar{\rho}_{m,h}\Pi_{m}\left(\mathbb{F}\left(\bar{\rho}_{m,h},\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{m,h}\right)\right)\right)\varphi(t)\psi(x)\operatorname{d}\operatorname{d}x\bar{W}_{m,h}

\displaystyle\rightarrow\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\Pi_{m}\left(\rho_{m,R}\Pi_{m}\left(\mathbb{F}\left(\rho_{m,R},\mathbf{u}_{m,R}\right)\right)\right)\varphi(t)\psi(x)\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}W_{m,R},\quad t\in[0,T],\quad\bar{\mathbb{P}}\text{ a.s.}\qquad\quad\square

2.2.2.
Energy balance and energy estimates for the regularized system

We first derive the energy estimates uniformly in
R
for regularized system (
2.1
). We use the general notation
\rho
and
\mathbf{u}
to take place of the notation
\rho_{m,R}
and
\mathbf{u}_{m,R}
in the process of the a priori energy estimates.

We apply Itô’s formula to the functional

(2.40)

\displaystyle g:L^{\gamma}\left(\mathbb{T}^{3}\right)\times H_{m}^{*}\rightarrow\mathds{R},\quad(\rho,\mathbf{q})\mapsto\frac{1}{2}\mathbf{q}\mathscr{M}^{-1}\left(\rho\right)\mathbf{q},

where
\mathbf{q}\in H_{m}^{*}
, and
\mathscr{M}^{-1}\left(\rho\right)
maps
H_{m}^{*}
to
H_{m}
. Then it holds

(2.41)

\displaystyle\partial_{\mathbf{q}}g\left(\rho,\mathbf{q}\right)=\mathscr{M}^{-1}\left(\rho\right)\mathbf{q}\in H_{m},\quad\partial^{2}_{\mathbf{q}}g\left(\rho,\mathbf{q}\right)=\mathscr{M}^{-1}\left(\rho\right),

\displaystyle\partial_{\rho}g\left(\rho,\mathbf{q}\right)=-\frac{1}{2}\mathscr{M}^{-1}\left(\rho\right)\left(\mathbf{q}\cdot\mathscr{M}^{-1}\left(\rho\right)\mathbf{q}\right)\in\mathds{R}.

Therefore, for
g=\frac{1}{2}\rho|\mathbf{u}|^{2}=\frac{1}{2}\frac{|\mathbf{q}|^{2}}{\rho}
, according to Itô’s formula (see §
5.2
in Appendix),
we deduce

(2.42)

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\left(\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\frac{1}{2}\rho|\mathbf{u}|^{2}\operatorname{d}x\right)=\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}|\mathbf{u}|^{2}\mathrm{d}\rho\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\mathbf{u}\cdot\operatorname{d}\left(\rho\mathbf{u}\right)\mathrm{d}x+\left(\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\rho\left|\mathbb{F}(\rho,\mathbf{u})\right|^{2}\mathrm{d}x\right)\mathrm{d}t.

Here and hereafter
"\operatorname{d}"
outside the integral in the above equality means differentiation with respect to time
t
.
Substituting expression of
\operatorname{d}\left(\rho\mathbf{u}\right)
of momentum equation into the above formula, it holds

\displaystyle\mathrm{d}\left(\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\frac{1}{2}\rho|\mathbf{u}|^{2}\operatorname{d}x\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}|\mathbf{u}|^{2}\mathrm{d}\rho\mathrm{d}x+\left(\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\rho\mathbb{F}(\rho,\mathbf{u})\cdot\mathbf{u}\mathrm{d}x\right)\mathrm{d}W+\left(\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\rho\left|\mathbb{F}(\rho,\mathbf{u})\right|^{2}\mathrm{d}x\right)\mathrm{d}t

(2.43)

\displaystyle-\left(\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\mathbf{u}\cdot\left(\operatorname{div}\left(\rho\mathbf{u}\otimes\mathbf{u}\right)+\nabla p\left(\rho\right)\right)\mathrm{d}x-\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\varepsilon\nabla\rho\nabla\mathbf{u}\cdot\mathbf{u}\mathrm{d}x-\varepsilon\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\left|\triangle\mathbf{u}\right|^{2}\mathrm{d}x\right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.+\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\operatorname{div}\left(\rho\mathbb{D}\mathbf{u}\right)\mathbf{u}\mathrm{d}x+\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\frac{11}{10}\eta\nabla\rho^{-10}\cdot\mathbf{u}\mathrm{d}x+\delta\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\rho\nabla\triangle^{9}\rho\cdot\mathbf{u}\mathrm{d}x\right.

\displaystyle\quad\left.-\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}r_{0}|\mathbf{u}|^{2}\mathbf{u}\cdot\mathbf{u}\operatorname{d}x-\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}r_{1}\rho|\mathbf{u}|^{2}\mathbf{u}\cdot\mathbf{u}\mathrm{d}x-\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}r_{2}\mathbf{u}\cdot\mathbf{u}\operatorname{d}x+\kappa\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\rho\nabla\left(\frac{\triangle\sqrt{\rho}}{\sqrt{\rho}}\right)\cdot\mathbf{u}\mathrm{d}x\right)\mathrm{d}t.

Since
\left(\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\mathbf{u}\cdot\operatorname{div}\left(\rho\mathbf{u}\otimes\mathbf{u}\right)\operatorname{d}x\right)\operatorname{d}t=\left(\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\operatorname{div}\left(\rho\mathbf{u}\right)\frac{1}{2}|\mathbf{u}|^{2}\operatorname{d}x\right)\operatorname{d}t,

we deduce that

(2.44)

-\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}|\mathbf{u}|^{2}\mathrm{d}\rho\operatorname{d}x-\left(\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\operatorname{div}\left(\rho\mathbf{u}\otimes\mathbf{u}\right)\cdot\mathbf{u}\operatorname{d}x\right)\operatorname{d}t-\left(\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\varepsilon\nabla\rho\nabla\mathbf{u}\cdot\mathbf{u}\operatorname{d}x\right)\operatorname{d}t=0.

Multiplying
-\eta\rho_{\eta}^{-11}
to the mass conservation equation, and integrating on
\mathbb{T}^{3}
, we have

(2.45)

\begin{array}[]{l}\left(\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\frac{11}{10}\eta\nabla\rho^{-10}\cdot\mathbf{u}\operatorname{d}x\right)\operatorname{d}t=-\operatorname{d}\left(\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\frac{\eta}{10}\rho^{-10}\operatorname{d}x\right)-\varepsilon\eta\frac{11}{25}\left(\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}|\nabla\rho^{-5}|^{2}\operatorname{d}x\right)\operatorname{d}t.\end{array}

Similarly, we have

(2.46)

-\left(\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\nabla p\cdot\mathbf{u}\operatorname{d}x\right)\operatorname{d}t=-\operatorname{d}\left(\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\frac{p}{\gamma-1}\operatorname{d}x\right)-\varepsilon\frac{4a}{\gamma}\left(\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}|\nabla(\rho^{\frac{\gamma}{2}})|^{2}\operatorname{d}x\right)\operatorname{d}t,\\

(2.47)

-\left(\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\delta\rho\nabla\triangle^{9}\rho\cdot\mathbf{u}\operatorname{d}x\right)\operatorname{d}t=-\operatorname{d}\left(\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\frac{\delta}{2}|\nabla\triangle^{4}\rho|^{2}\operatorname{d}x\right)-\varepsilon\delta\left(\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}|\triangle^{5}\rho|^{2}\operatorname{d}x\right)\operatorname{d}t.\\

For the term
\kappa\rho\nabla\left(\frac{\triangle\sqrt{\rho}}{\sqrt{\rho}}\right)
, we use the equality relation
2\rho\nabla\left(\frac{\triangle\sqrt{\rho}}{\sqrt{\rho}}\right)=\operatorname{div}\left(\rho\nabla^{2}\ln\rho\right)
. Therefore, we have

(2.48)

\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\kappa\rho\nabla\left(\frac{\triangle\sqrt{\rho}}{\sqrt{\rho}}\right)\cdot\mathbf{u}\operatorname{d}x=-\kappa\frac{\operatorname{d}}{\operatorname{d}t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}|\nabla\sqrt{\rho}|^{2}\operatorname{d}x-\kappa\varepsilon\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\rho|\nabla^{2}\ln\rho|^{2}\operatorname{d}x.

To conclude, we obtain the energy balance

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\left(\frac{1}{2}\rho|\mathbf{u}|^{2}+\frac{a}{\gamma}\rho^{\gamma}+\frac{\eta}{10}\rho^{-10}+\kappa|\nabla\sqrt{\rho}|^{2}+\frac{\delta}{2}|\nabla\triangle^{4}\rho|^{2}\right)\operatorname{d}x

\displaystyle+\varepsilon\frac{4a}{\gamma}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}|\nabla\rho^{\frac{\gamma}{2}}|^{2}\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}s+\varepsilon\eta\frac{11}{25}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}|\nabla\rho^{-5}|^{2}\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}s

(2.49)

\displaystyle+\varepsilon\delta\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}|\triangle^{5}\rho|^{4}\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}s+\frac{\varepsilon\kappa}{2}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\rho|\nabla^{2}\ln\rho|^{2}\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}t+\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\rho|\mathbb{D}\mathbf{u}|^{2}\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}s

\displaystyle+\varepsilon\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\left|\triangle\mathbf{u}\right|^{2}\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}s+r_{0}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}|\mathbf{u}|^{4}\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}s+r_{1}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\rho|\mathbf{u}|^{4}\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}s+r_{2}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}|\mathbf{u}|^{2}\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}s

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\left(\frac{1}{2}\frac{|\mathbf{q}_{0}|^{2}}{\rho_{0}}+\frac{a}{\gamma}\rho_{0}^{\gamma}+\frac{\eta}{10}\rho_{0}^{-10}+\kappa|\nabla\sqrt{\rho_{0}}|^{2}+\frac{\delta}{2}|\nabla\triangle^{4}\rho_{0}|^{2}\right)\operatorname{d}x

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\rho|\mathbb{F}_{k}(\rho,\mathbf{u})|^{2}\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}s+\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\rho\mathbb{F}_{k}(\rho,\mathbf{u})\cdot\mathbf{u}\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}W.

The
C_{r}
inequality states that: for real-valued
r>1
,
a
and
b
, by the property of convex functions, it holds
|a+b|^{r}\leqslant\max(1,2^{r-1})(|a|^{r}+|b|^{r})
. Hence to estimate
r-
th expectation of the right-hand side, for some
r\geqslant 1
, we only need to consider

(2.50)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\rho|\mathbb{F}(\rho,\mathbf{u})|^{2}\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}s\right|^{r}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\sum\limits_{k=1}^{+\infty}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\rho\left|\mathbf{F}_{k}(\rho,\mathbf{u})\right|^{2}\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}s\right|^{r}\right]

and

(2.51)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\rho\mathbb{F}\left(\rho,\mathbf{u}\right)\cdot\mathbf{u}\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}W\right|^{r}\right]\leqslant C\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\int_{0}^{t}\left|\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\rho\sum\limits_{k=1}^{+\infty}\mathbf{F}_{k}\left(\rho,\mathbf{u}\right)e_{k}\cdot\mathbf{u}\operatorname{d}x\right|^{2}\operatorname{d}s\right|^{\frac{r}{2}}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\sum\limits_{k=1}^{+\infty}\int_{0}^{t}\left|\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\rho\mathbf{F}_{k}\left(\rho,\mathbf{u}\right)\cdot\mathbf{u}\operatorname{d}x\right|^{2}\operatorname{d}s\right|^{\frac{r}{2}}\right]

respectively, where
C
depends on
r
.

For any function
\xi
,
1<\gamma<3
,
\xi^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}<(1+\xi)^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}<(1+\xi)
. By the property of
\mathbf{F}_{k}
, we do the following energy estimates,

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{k=1}^{+\infty}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\rho\left|\Pi_{m}\left(\mathbf{F}_{k}(\rho,\mathbf{u})\right)\right|^{2}\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}t\leqslant\sum\limits_{k=1}^{+\infty}\sum\limits_{k=1}^{+\infty}f^{2}_{k}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\frac{\left(\left|\rho\right|+\left|\rho\mathbf{u}\right|\right)^{2}}{\rho}\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}s

(2.52)

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle C\left\|\rho\right\|_{L_{t}^{1}L_{x}^{1}}+C\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\rho\left|\mathbf{u}\right|^{2}\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}s\leqslant C\int_{0}^{t}\left(\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\rho^{\gamma}\operatorname{d}x\right)^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}\operatorname{d}s+C\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\rho\left|\mathbf{u}\right|^{2}\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}s

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle C\int_{0}^{t}\left(\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\rho^{\gamma}\operatorname{d}x+1\right)\operatorname{d}s+C\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\rho\left|\mathbf{u}\right|^{2}\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}s,\quad t\in\left[0,T\right],

uniformly in
R
and
m
, where
C
depends on
\sum\limits_{k=1}^{+\infty}f^{2}_{k}
. By Jensen’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\quad\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\sum\limits_{k=1}^{+\infty}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\rho\left|\Pi_{m}\left(\mathbf{F}_{k}(\rho,\mathbf{u})\right)\right|^{2}\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}s\right|^{r}\right]

(2.53)

\displaystyle\leqslant C\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{0}^{t}\left(\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\left(\rho\left|\mathbf{u}\right|^{2}+\rho^{\gamma}\right)(s,x)\operatorname{d}x+1\right)\operatorname{d}s\right)^{r}\right]

\displaystyle\leqslant C\mathbb{E}\left[t^{r}\int_{0}^{1}\left(\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\left(\rho\left|\mathbf{u}\right|^{2}+\rho^{\gamma}\right)(ts,x)\operatorname{d}x+1\right)^{r}\operatorname{d}s\right]

\displaystyle\leqslant C\left(t^{r-1}\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{t}\left(\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\left(\rho\left|\mathbf{u}\right|^{2}+\rho^{\gamma}\right)(s,x)\operatorname{d}x\right)^{r}\operatorname{d}s\right]+t^{r}\right),\quad t\in[0,T],

for some
r\geqslant 1
, where
C
depends on
r
and
\sum\limits_{k=1}^{+\infty}f^{2}_{k}
.
For (
2.51
),

\frac{1}{2\gamma}+\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{q}=1
, it holds

(2.54)

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{k=1}^{+\infty}\left|\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\rho\Pi_{m}\left(\mathbf{F}_{k}\left(\rho,\mathbf{u}\right)\right)\cdot\mathbf{u}\operatorname{d}x\right|^{2}\leqslant C\sum\limits_{k=1}^{+\infty}f_{k}^{2}\left|\left\|\sqrt{\rho}\right\|_{L_{x}^{2\gamma}}\left\|\sqrt{\rho}\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L_{x}^{2}}+\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\rho\left|\mathbf{u}\right|^{2}\operatorname{d}x\right|^{2},

(2.55)

\displaystyle\left|\left\|\sqrt{\rho}\right\|_{L_{x}^{2\gamma}}\left\|\sqrt{\rho}\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L_{x}^{2}}\right|^{2}\leqslant C\left|\left\|\sqrt{\rho}\right\|_{L_{x}^{2\gamma}}\left\|\sqrt{\rho}\mathbf{u}\right\|_{L_{x}^{2}}\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle C\left(\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\rho^{\gamma}\operatorname{d}x\right)^{\frac{1}{\gamma}}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\rho\mathbf{u}^{2}\operatorname{d}x\leqslant C\left(\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\rho^{\gamma}\operatorname{d}x+1\right)\left(\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\rho\mathbf{u}^{2}\operatorname{d}x\right),

where
C
depends on
\sum\limits_{k=1}^{+\infty}f^{2}_{k}
.

Due to the property of convex functions, for some
r\geqslant 2
, by Jensen’s inequality, we have

\displaystyle\quad\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\rho\Pi_{m}\left(\mathbb{F}\left(\rho\mathbf{u}\right)\right)\cdot\mathbf{u}\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}W\right|^{r}\right]

(2.56)

\displaystyle\leqslant C\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{0}^{t}\left(\left(\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\rho^{\gamma}(s)\operatorname{d}x+\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\rho(s)\mathbf{u}^{2}(s)\operatorname{d}x\right)^{2}+1\right)\operatorname{d}s\right)^{\frac{r}{2}}\right]

\displaystyle=C\mathbb{E}\left[\left(t\int_{0}^{1}\left(\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\rho^{\gamma}(ts)\operatorname{d}x+\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\rho(ts)\mathbf{u}^{2}(ts)\operatorname{d}x\right)^{2}\operatorname{d}s+t\right)^{\frac{r}{2}}\right]

\displaystyle\leqslant C\left(t^{\frac{r}{2}-1}\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\rho^{\gamma}(s)\operatorname{d}x+\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\rho(s,x)\mathbf{u}^{2}(s)\operatorname{d}x\right)^{r}\right]\operatorname{d}s+t^{\frac{r}{2}}\right),

uniformly in
R
and
m
, where
C
depends on
r
. Now we recover the notation.
Let

(2.57)

e_{m,R}(t)=\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\left(\frac{1}{2}\rho_{m,R}|\mathbf{u}_{m,R}|^{2}+\frac{a}{\gamma}\rho_{m,R}^{\gamma}+\frac{\eta}{10}\rho_{m,R}^{-10}+\frac{\kappa}{2}|\nabla\sqrt{\rho_{m,R}}|^{2}+\frac{\delta}{2}|\nabla\triangle^{4}\rho_{m,R}|^{2}\right)\operatorname{d}x.\\

Combining the above estimates for the stochastic integrals, therefore, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left(e_{m,R}\left(t\right)+\varepsilon\frac{4a}{\gamma}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\left|\nabla\rho_{m,R}^{\frac{\gamma}{2}}\right|^{2}\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}s+\varepsilon\eta\frac{11}{25}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\left|\nabla\rho_{m,R}^{-5}\right|^{2}\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}s\right.\right.

\displaystyle+\left.\left.\varepsilon\delta\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}|\triangle^{5}\rho_{m,R}|^{2}\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}s+\varepsilon\kappa\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\rho_{m,R}|\nabla^{2}\ln\rho_{m,R}|^{2}\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}s+\varepsilon\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\left|\triangle\mathbf{u}_{m,R}\right|^{2}\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}s\right.\right.

(2.58)

\displaystyle+\left.\left.\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\rho_{m,R}|\mathbb{D}\mathbf{u}_{m,R}|^{2}\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}s+r_{0}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}|\mathbf{u}_{m,R}|^{4}\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}s\right.\right.

\displaystyle+\left.\left.r_{1}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\rho_{m,R}|\mathbf{u}_{m,R}|^{4}\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}s+r_{2}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}|\mathbf{u}_{m,R}|^{2}\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}s\right)^{r}\right]

\displaystyle\leqslant C\left((t^{\frac{r}{2}-1}+t^{r-1})\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}\left[e_{m,R}(s)^{r}\right]\operatorname{d}s+t^{\frac{r}{2}}+t^{r}+\mathbb{E}\left[e_{m,R}(0)^{r}\right]\right).

Hence it holds

(2.59)

\quad\mathbb{E}[\left(e_{m,R}(t)\right)^{r}]\leqslant C\left((t^{\frac{r}{2}-1}+t^{r-1})\int_{0}^{t}\mathbb{E}\left[e_{m,R}(s)^{r}\right]\operatorname{d}s+t^{\frac{r}{2}}+t^{r}+\mathbb{E}\left[e_{m,R}(0)^{r}\right]\right),

where
C
depends on
r
and
\sum\limits_{k=1}^{+\infty}f^{2}_{k}
.
By Grönwall’s inequality,
t^{\frac{r}{2}}+t^{r}+\mathbb{E}\left[e_{m,R}(0)^{r}\right]
is non-decreasing with respect to
t
, it holds

(2.60)

\mathbb{E}[\sup\limits_{t\in[0,T]}\left(e_{m,R}(t)\right)^{r}]\leqslant C\left(t^{\frac{r}{2}}+t^{r}+\mathbb{E}\left[e_{m,R}(0)^{r}\right]\right)e^{C(t^{\frac{r}{2}}+t^{r})}\leqslant C(\mathbb{E}\left[e_{m,R}(0)^{r}\right]+1),

where
C
depends on
r

T
, and
\sum\limits_{k=1}^{+\infty}f^{2}_{k}
,
r\geqslant 2
.
Thereupon, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\varepsilon\frac{4a}{\gamma}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}|\nabla\rho_{m,R}^{\frac{\gamma}{2}}|^{2}\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}s+\varepsilon\eta\frac{11}{25}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}|\nabla\rho_{m,R}^{-5}|^{2}\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}s\right.\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.\quad+\varepsilon\delta\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}|\triangle^{5}\rho_{m,R}|^{2}\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}s+\varepsilon\kappa\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\rho_{m,R}|\nabla^{2}\ln\rho_{m,R}|^{2}\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}s\right.\right.

(2.61)

\displaystyle\left.\left.\quad+\varepsilon\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\left|\triangle\mathbf{u}_{m,R}\right|^{2}\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\rho_{m,R}|\mathbb{D}\mathbf{u}_{m,R}|^{2}\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}s\right.\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.\quad+r_{0}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}|\mathbf{u}_{m,R}|^{4}\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}s+r_{1}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\rho_{m,R}|\mathbf{u}_{m,R}|^{4}\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}s+r_{2}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}|\mathbf{u}_{m,R}|^{2}\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}s\right)^{r}\right]

\displaystyle\leqslant C(\mathbb{E}\left[e_{m,R}(0)^{r}\right]+1),

where
C
depends on
r

T
, and
\sum\limits_{k=1}^{+\infty}f^{2}_{k}
,
r\geqslant 2
.

2.2.3.
The global existence of the classical solutions to the nonlinear regularized Galerkin scheme in
H_{m}

We define
\tau_{R}
, a stoping time (see §
5.1
in Appendix), until which
\left(\rho_{m,R},\mathbf{u}_{m,R}\right)
is the well-defined in
C\left([0,T];H_{m}\right)\times C^{0,\nu}\left([0,T];H_{m}\right)
,

(2.62)

\tau_{R}=\inf\left\{t\in[0,T]\left|\left\|\mathbf{u}_{m,R}\right\|_{H_{m}}>R\right.\right\}.

For this layer
R\rightarrow+\infty
, we need show that

(2.63)

\bar{\mathbb{P}}\left[\left\{\sup\limits_{R\in\mathds{N}}\tau_{R}=T\right\}\right]=1.

In other words, till time
T
, the solutions will not blow up as
R\rightarrow\infty
.
Due to (
2.6
), we only need consider
\left\|\mathbf{u}_{m,R}\right\|_{H_{m}}
and then the well-posedness of
\rho_{m,R}
follows.
From (
2.2.2
) we know that
\left\|\triangle\mathbf{u}_{m,R}\right\|_{L_{t}^{2}L_{x}^{2}}\leqslant C
,
\bar{\mathbb{P}}
a.s., this implies that
\left\|\nabla\mathbf{u}_{m,R}\right\|_{L_{t}^{2}L_{x}^{6}}\leqslant C
,
\bar{\mathbb{P}}
a.s., where
C
depends on
r
,
T
, and
\sum\limits_{k=1}^{+\infty}f^{2}_{k}
. Since
\mathbb{E}\left[\left(r_{2}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}|\mathbf{u}_{m,R}|^{2}\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}s\right)^{r}\right]\leqslant C(\mathbb{E}\left[e_{m,R}(0)^{r}\right]+1)
, it holds

(2.64)

\left\|\mathbf{u}_{m,R}\right\|_{H_{m}}\leqslant C,\quad\left\|\operatorname{div}\mathbf{u}_{m,R}\right\|_{H_{m}}\leqslant C,\quad\bar{\mathbb{P}}\quad\text{a.s.}

This shows that
\tau_{R}=T
for
R
large enough.

2.3.
m
goes to infinity

For the space of
\mathbf{u}_{m}
, we try not to use the regular property from
\triangle^{2}\mathbf{u}_{m}
because
\varepsilon
converges to zero finally.

Let
R\rightarrow\infty
in the energy estimates (
2.57
), (
2.60
) and (
2.2.2
), then we have

(2.65)

\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\varepsilon\kappa\left\|\rho_{m}^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\|_{L_{t}^{2}H^{2}_{x}}\right)^{r}\right]\leqslant C,\quad\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\varepsilon\kappa\left\|\nabla\left(\rho_{m}^{\frac{1}{4}}\right)\right\|_{L_{t}^{4}L_{x}^{4}}^{2}\right)^{r}\right]\leqslant C,

where
C
depends on
T,r
,
\sum\limits_{k=1}^{+\infty}f^{2}_{k}
and
\mathbb{E}\left[e_{m}(0)^{r}\right]
.
To prove it, we used the following lemma which is from Jüngle’s original idea
[
26
]
.

Lemma 2.2
.

For any smooth positive function
f(x)
, it holds

(2.66)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}f\left|\nabla^{2}\ln f\right|^{2}\operatorname{d}x\geqslant\frac{1}{7}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\left|\nabla^{2}f^{\frac{1}{2}}\right|^{2}\operatorname{d}x+\frac{1}{8}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\left|\nabla f^{\frac{1}{4}}\right|^{4}\operatorname{d}x.

To show the convergence of
\rho_{m}
and
\mathbf{u}_{m}
, we will proceed in the following four steps.

Step 1:
Choose the path space.

We will roughly give the estimates for
\rho_{m}
,
\rho_{m}\mathbf{u}_{m}
, and
\rho_{m}^{\frac{1}{2}}
.

Lemma 2.3
.

For some
r\geqslant 2
, there exists a constant
C
such that

(2.67)

\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\left\|(\rho_{m})_{t}\right\|_{L_{t}^{2}L_{x}^{\frac{3}{2}}}\right)^{r}\right]\leqslant C,\quad\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\left\|\left(\rho_{m}^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)_{t}\right\|_{L_{t}^{2}L_{x}^{2}}\right)^{r}\right]\leqslant C,\quad\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\left\|\rho_{m}\mathbf{u}_{m}\right\|_{L_{t}^{2}L_{x}^{2}}\right)^{r}\right]\leqslant C,

(2.68)

\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\left\|\rho_{m}^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\|_{L_{t}^{2}H_{x}^{2}}\right)^{r}\right]\leqslant C,\quad\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\left\|\nabla\left(\rho_{m}^{\frac{1}{4}}\right)\right\|_{L_{t}^{4}L_{x}^{4}}\right)^{r}\right]\leqslant C.\quad\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\left\|\rho_{m}^{\gamma}\right\|_{L_{t}^{\frac{5}{3}}L_{x}^{\frac{5}{3}}}\right)^{r}\right]\leqslant C,

where
C
is merely dependent on
T,r,r_{1},\delta,\varepsilon\kappa
, and (
2.67
) will be independent on
\delta
after
\varepsilon\rightarrow 0
.

Proof.

Recall that

(2.69)

\displaystyle(\rho_{m})_{t}

\displaystyle=-\rho_{m}\operatorname{div}\mathbf{u}_{m}-\nabla\rho_{m}\cdot\mathbf{u}_{m}+\varepsilon\triangle\rho_{m}

\displaystyle=-\rho_{m}^{\frac{1}{2}}\rho_{m}^{\frac{1}{2}}\operatorname{div}\mathbf{u}_{m}-\left(\rho_{m}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{u}_{m}\right)\cdot\left(2\nabla\rho_{m}^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)+\varepsilon\triangle\rho_{m}.

On one hand, for some
r\geqslant 2
,

(2.70)

\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\rho_{m}^{\frac{1}{2}}\rho_{m}^{\frac{1}{2}}\operatorname{div}\mathbf{u}_{m}\right\|_{L_{t}^{2}L_{x}^{\frac{3}{2}}}^{r}\right]\leqslant\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\left\|\rho_{m}^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}L_{x}^{6}}\left\|\rho_{m}^{\frac{1}{2}}\operatorname{div}\mathbf{u}_{m}\right\|_{L_{t}^{2}L_{x}^{2}}\right)^{r}\right]\leqslant C.

On the other hand,

(2.71)

\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\nabla\rho_{m}^{\frac{1}{2}}\cdot\left(\rho_{m}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{u}_{m}\right)\right\|_{L_{t}^{2}L_{x}^{\frac{3}{2}}}^{r}\right]\leqslant\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\left\|\rho_{m}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{u}_{m}\right\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}L_{x}^{2}}\left\|\nabla\rho_{m}^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\|_{L_{t}^{2}L_{x}^{6}}\right)^{r}\right]\leqslant C,

so

(2.72)

\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\left\|(\rho_{m})_{t}\right\|_{L_{t}^{2}L_{x}^{\frac{3}{2}}}\right)^{r}\right]\leqslant C,

C
depends on
T,r,\varepsilon
and
\kappa
.
Meanwhile, the mass equation reduces to

(2.73)

\displaystyle 2\left(\rho_{m}^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)_{t}

\displaystyle=-\rho_{m}^{\frac{1}{2}}\operatorname{div}\mathbf{u}_{m}-2\nabla\rho_{m}^{\frac{1}{2}}\cdot\mathbf{u}_{m}+\varepsilon\frac{\triangle\rho_{m}}{\rho_{m}^{\frac{1}{2}}}.

Letting
R\rightarrow\infty
in the energy estimates (
2.57
), (
2.60
) and (
2.2.2
), and by
H^{8}\left(\mathbb{T}^{3}\right)\hookrightarrow C^{6,\frac{1}{2}}\left(\mathbb{T}^{3}\right)
, we have

(2.74)

\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\left\|\varepsilon\frac{\triangle\rho_{m}}{\rho_{m}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\right\|_{L_{t}^{2}L_{x}^{2}}\right)^{r}\right]\leqslant C,

where
C
depends on
T,r,\varepsilon\eta
and
\varepsilon\delta
.
Hence we obtain

(2.75)

\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\left\|\left(\rho_{m}^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)_{t}\right\|_{L_{t}^{2}L_{x}^{2}}\right)^{r}\right]\leqslant C,

for
C
depends on
T,r,r_{1},\varepsilon\eta,\varepsilon\delta
and
