\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\nabla_{\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{{\rm I}}}\varphi_{n}(\bar{\mathbf{v}}_{{\rm I}})\phi_{K}(\bar{\rho}_{{\rm I}})\bar{\rho}_{{\rm I}}\mathbb{F}(\bar{\rho}_{{\rm I}},\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{{\rm I}})\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}\bar{W}_{{\rm I}}\rightarrow\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\nabla_{\mathbf{u}_{{\rm II}}}\varphi_{n}(\mathbf{u}_{{\rm II}})\rho_{{\rm II}}\mathbb{F}(\rho_{{\rm II}},\mathbf{u}_{{\rm II}})\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}W_{{\rm II}}.

\bar{\mathbb{P}}
a.s. as
\kappa\rightarrow 0
. Therefore, we have the following lemma.

Lemma 4.1
.

\left(\rho_{{\rm II}},\mathbf{u}_{{\rm II}}\right)
satisfies

(4.51)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\rho_{{\rm II}}\varphi_{n}(\mathbf{u}_{{\rm II}})\operatorname{d}x=-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\nabla_{\mathbf{u}_{{\rm II}}}\varphi_{n}(\mathbf{u}_{{\rm II}})\mathbf{R}_{{\rm II}}\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}s+\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\rho_{0,{\rm II}}\varphi_{n}\left(\frac{\mathbf{q}_{0,{\rm II}}}{\rho_{0,{\rm II}}}\right)\operatorname{d}x

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\rho_{{\rm II}}^{\gamma}\nabla_{\mathbf{u}_{{\rm II}}}^{2}\varphi_{n}(\mathbf{u}_{{\rm II}}):\nabla\mathbf{u}_{{\rm II}}\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\rho_{{\rm II}}\nabla_{\mathbf{u}_{{\rm II}}}^{2}\varphi_{n}(\mathbf{u}_{{\rm II}})\nabla\mathbf{u}_{{\rm II}}:\mathbb{D}\mathbf{u}_{{\rm II}}\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}s

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\rho_{{\rm II}}\mathbb{F}(\rho_{{\rm II}},\mathbf{u}_{{\rm II}})\nabla_{\mathbf{u}_{{\rm II}}}^{2}\varphi_{n}(\mathbf{u}_{{\rm II}})\mathbb{F}(\rho_{{\rm II}},\mathbf{u}_{{\rm II}})\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}s+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\nabla_{\mathbf{u}_{{\rm II}}}\varphi_{n}(\mathbf{u}_{{\rm II}})\rho_{{\rm II}}\mathbb{F}(\rho_{{\rm II}},\mathbf{u}_{{\rm II}})\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}W_{{\rm II}},

and the estimate

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\int_{\Omega}\rho_{{\rm II}}\varphi_{n}(\mathbf{u}_{{\rm II}})\operatorname{d}x+\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\nabla_{\mathbf{u}_{{\rm II}}}\varphi_{n}(\mathbf{u}_{{\rm II}})\mathbf{R}_{{\rm II}}\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}s\right|^{r}\right]

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle\bar{C}+C_{r}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\int_{\Omega}\rho_{0,{\rm II}}\varphi_{n}\left(\frac{\mathbf{q}_{0,{\rm II}}}{\rho_{0,{\rm II}}}\right)\operatorname{d}x\right|^{r}\right]

(4.52)

\displaystyle+C_{r}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\rho_{{\rm II}}\mathbb{F}(\rho_{{\rm II}},\mathbf{u}_{{\rm II}})\nabla_{\mathbf{u}_{{\rm II}}}^{2}\varphi_{n}\left(\mathbf{u}_{{\rm II}}\right)\mathbb{F}(\rho_{{\rm II}},\mathbf{u}_{{\rm II}})\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}s\right|^{r}\right]

\displaystyle+C_{r}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\left(1+\tilde{\varphi}_{n}^{\prime}\left(|\mathbf{u}_{{\rm II}}|^{2}\right)\right)\rho_{{\rm II}}^{2\gamma-1}\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}s\right|^{r}\right]

\displaystyle+C_{r}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\int_{0}^{t}\nabla_{\mathbf{u}_{{\rm II}}}\varphi_{n}(\mathbf{u}_{{\rm II}})\rho_{{\rm II}}\mathbb{F}(\rho_{{\rm II}},\mathbf{u}_{{\rm II}})\operatorname{d}W_{{\rm II}}\operatorname{d}x\right|^{r}\right],

where

(4.53)

\mathbf{R}_{{\rm II}}=\eta\nabla\rho_{{\rm II}}^{-10}+\delta\rho_{{\rm II}}\nabla\triangle^{9}\rho_{{\rm II}}+r_{0}|\mathbf{u}_{{\rm II}}|^{2}\mathbf{u}_{{\rm II}}+r_{1}\rho_{{\rm II}}|\mathbf{u}_{{\rm II}}|^{2}\mathbf{u}_{{\rm II}}+r_{2}\mathbf{u}_{{\rm II}},

\bar{C}
depends on
r,T
, and
\mathbb{E}\left[e_{{\rm I}}(0)^{r}\right]
,
C_{r}
merely depends on
r
.

Proposition 4.3
.

\left(\left(\bar{\Omega},\bar{\mathcal{F}},\bar{\mathbb{P}}\right),\rho_{{\rm II}},\mathbf{u}_{{\rm II}},W_{{\rm II}}\right)
is a martingale solution to

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\vskip 5.16663pt\left(\rho_{{\rm II}}\right)_{t}+\operatorname{div}(\rho_{{\rm II}}\mathbf{u}_{{\rm II}})=0,\\
\operatorname{d}\left(\rho_{{\rm II}}\mathbf{u}_{{\rm II}}\right)+\left(\operatorname{div}(\rho_{{\rm II}}\mathbf{u}_{{\rm II}}\otimes\mathbf{u}_{{\rm II}})+\nabla\left(a\rho_{{\rm II}}^{\gamma}\right)-\operatorname{div}(\rho_{{\rm II}}\mathbb{D}\mathbf{u}_{{\rm II}})-\frac{11}{10}\eta\nabla\rho_{{\rm II}}^{-10}\right)\operatorname{d}t\\
=(-r_{0}|\mathbf{u}_{{\rm II}}|^{2}\mathbf{u}_{{\rm II}}-r_{1}\rho_{{\rm II}}|\mathbf{u}_{{\rm II}}|^{2}\mathbf{u}_{{\rm II}}-r_{2}\mathbf{u}_{{\rm II}})\operatorname{d}t+\delta\rho_{{\rm II}}\nabla\triangle^{9}\rho_{{\rm II}}\operatorname{d}t+\rho_{{\rm II}}\mathbb{F}(\rho_{{\rm II}},\mathbf{u}_{{\rm II}})\operatorname{d}W_{{\rm II}}.\end{array}\right.

Proof.

The weak convergence of
\nabla\bar{\rho}_{{\rm I}}^{\frac{\gamma}{2}}
,

\bar{\rho}_{{\rm I}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla^{2}\ln\bar{\rho}_{{\rm I}}
still holds in the corresponding spaces. By Vitali’s convergence theorem, the limits of
\bar{\rho}_{{\rm I}}
and
\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{{\rm I}}
satisfy the mass conservation equation. We just consider the convergence of stochastic forces.
We show that what is limit of stochastic integral
\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\bar{\rho}_{{\rm I}}\mathbb{F}(\bar{\rho}_{{\rm I}},\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{{\rm I}})\operatorname{d}\bar{W}_{{\rm I}}\operatorname{d}x
.

By (
4.19
) and (
4.21
), we have

(4.57)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\left|\bar{\rho}_{{\rm I}}\mathbb{F}(\bar{\rho}_{{\rm I}},\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{{\rm I}})\right|^{2}\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}t\leqslant C,

where
C
depends on
T,r,\mathbb{E}\left[e(0)^{r}\right]
and
\mathbb{E}\left[\tilde{e}(0)^{r}\right]
.
In addition, we have derived the strong convergence of
\bar{\rho}_{{\rm I}}
in
L^{2}\left(0,T;L^{\frac{3}{2}}\left(\mathbb{T}^{3}\right)\right)
and the strong convergence of
\bar{\rho}_{{\rm I}}\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{{\rm I}}
in
L^{2}\left(0,T;L^{\frac{3}{2}}\left(\mathbb{T}^{3}\right)\right)
, hence we know
\bar{\rho}_{{\rm I}}\rightarrow\rho_{{\rm II}}
almost everywhere,
\bar{\rho}_{{\rm I}}\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{{\rm I}}\rightarrow\rho_{{\rm II}}\mathbf{u}_{{\rm II}}
almost everywhere up to a subsequence. Therefore
\bar{\rho}_{{\rm I}}\mathbb{F}\left(\bar{\rho}_{{\rm I}},\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{{\rm I}}\right)\rightarrow\rho_{{\rm II}}\mathbb{F}\left(\rho_{{\rm II}},\mathbf{u}_{{\rm II}}\right)
holds almost everywhere. By dominated convergence theorem, we have

(4.58)

\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\left|\bar{\rho}_{{\rm I}}\mathbb{F}\left(\bar{\rho}_{{\rm I}},\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{{\rm I}}\right)\right|^{2}\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}t\rightarrow\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\left|\rho_{{\rm II}}\mathbb{F}\left(\rho_{{\rm II}},\mathbf{u}_{{\rm II}}\right)\right|^{2}\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}t,\quad\bar{\mathbb{P}}\text{ a.s.}.

Then the convergence of the stochastic integral follows according to Lemma
2.4
.
\square

Moreover, letting

(4.59)

\displaystyle e_{{\rm II}}(t)=\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\left(\frac{1}{2}\rho_{{\rm II}}\left|\mathbf{u}_{{\rm II}}\right|^{2}+\frac{\eta}{10}\rho_{{\rm II}}^{-10}+\frac{\delta}{2}\left|\nabla\triangle^{4}\rho_{{\rm II}}\right|^{2}+\int_{1}^{\rho_{{\rm II}}}\frac{p(z)}{z}\operatorname{d}z\right)\operatorname{d}x,

(4.60)

\displaystyle\tilde{e}_{{\rm II}}(t)=\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\left(\frac{1}{2}\rho_{{\rm II}}\left|\mathbf{u}_{{\rm II}}+\nabla\ln\rho_{{\rm II}}\right|^{2}+\frac{\eta}{10}\rho_{{\rm II}}^{-10}+\frac{\delta}{2}\left|\nabla\triangle^{4}\rho_{{\rm II}}\right|^{2}+\int_{1}^{\rho_{{\rm II}}}\frac{p(z)}{z}\operatorname{d}z\right)\operatorname{d}x,

by Grönwall’s inequality, we have the following estimates,

(4.61)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sup\limits_{t\in[0,T]}e_{{\rm II}}(t)^{r}\right]\leqslant C,\quad\mathbb{E}\left[\sup\limits_{t\in[0,T]}\tilde{e}_{{\rm II}}(t)^{r}\right]\leqslant C,

where
C
depends on
r,T,\mathbb{E}\left[e_{{\rm II}}(0)^{r}\right]
,
\mathbb{E}\left[\tilde{e}_{{\rm II}}(0)^{r}\right]
,
r>4
.

4.3.
Vanishing the artificial pressure and Rayleigh damping:
\delta,\eta,r_{0}\rightarrow 0

We take
n\rightarrow+\infty
to derive a new stochastic Mellet-Vasseur type inequality. Recall that in the process of the stochastic B-D entropy estimates,
estimate of the term
r_{0}\mathbf{u}_{{\rm II}}^{3}
is dependent on
\delta
and
\eta
, which will cause trouble if we firstly take
\delta,\eta\rightarrow 0
. So we will take the limit
n\rightarrow\infty,\delta,\eta,r_{0}\rightarrow 0
at the same time, then we do the energy estimates for the system, which shows the terms like
\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\delta\mid\nabla\triangle^{4}\rho_{{\rm II}}\mid^{2}\operatorname{d}x\right)^{r}\right]
in the energy estimates will vanish after we take the limit
\delta\rightarrow 0
. So does the
\eta-
term in the stochastic B-D entropy.

Step 1:
Choose the path space and show the tightness of the laws and the convergence.

Take the space
\mathcal{X}_{5}=\mathcal{X}_{\rho_{0}}^{5}\times\mathcal{X}_{\frac{\mathbf{q}_{0}}{\sqrt{\rho_{0}}}}^{5}\times\mathcal{X}_{\mathbf{q}_{0}}^{5}\times\mathcal{X}_{\rho}^{5}\times\mathcal{X}_{\rho^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{u}}^{5}\times\mathcal{X}_{W}^{5}
,
where
\mathcal{X}_{\rho_{0}}^{5}=L^{\gamma}\left(\mathbb{T}^{3}\right)
,
\mathcal{X}_{\frac{\mathbf{q}_{0}}{\sqrt{\rho_{0}}}}^{5}=L^{2}\left(\mathbb{T}^{3}\right)
,

\mathcal{X}_{\mathbf{q}_{0}}^{5}=L^{1}\left(\mathbb{T}^{3}\right)
,

\mathcal{X}_{\rho}^{5}=L^{2}\left(0,T;L^{\frac{3}{2}}\left(\mathbb{T}^{3}\right)\right)\cap L^{\frac{5}{3}\gamma}\left([0,T]\times\mathbb{T}^{3}\right)
,

\mathcal{X}_{\rho^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{u}}^{5}=L^{2}\left([0,T]\times\mathbb{T}^{3}\right)
,

\mathcal{X}_{W}^{5}=C\left([0,T];\mathcal{H}\right)
. Then we have the corresponding tightness and convergence theorem.

Proposition 4.4
.

There exists two families of
\mathcal{X}_{5}
-valued Borel measurable random variables

\left\{\bar{\rho}_{0,{\rm II}},\frac{\bar{\mathbf{q}}_{0,{\rm II}}}{\sqrt{\bar{\rho}_{0,{\rm II}}}},\bar{\mathbf{q}}_{0,{\rm II}},\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}},\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{{\rm II}},\bar{W}_{{\rm II}}\right\}

and

\left\{\rho_{0,\rm{III}},\frac{\mathbf{q}_{0,{\rm III}}}{\sqrt{\rho_{0,{\rm III}}}},\mathbf{q}_{0,{\rm III}},\rho_{{\rm III}},\rho_{{\rm III}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{u}_{\rm{\tiny{III}}},W_{{\rm III}}\right\}
,
defined on a new complete probability space
\left(\bar{\Omega},\bar{\mathcal{F}},\bar{\mathbb{P}}\right)
, such that (up to a subsequence):

(1)

\mathcal{L}\left[\bar{\rho}_{0,{\rm II}},\frac{\bar{\mathbf{q}}_{0,{\rm II}}}{\sqrt{\bar{\rho}_{0,{\rm II}}}},\bar{\mathbf{q}}_{0,{\rm II}},\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}},\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{{\rm II}},\bar{W}_{{\rm II}}\right]
and

\mathcal{L}\left[\rho_{0,{\rm II}},\frac{\mathbf{q}_{0,{\rm II}}}{\sqrt{\rho_{0,{\rm II}}}},\mathbf{q}_{0,{\rm II}},\rho_{{\rm II}},\rho_{{\rm II}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{u}_{{\rm II}},W_{{\rm II}}\right]

coincides with each other on
\mathcal{X}_{5}
;

(2)

\mathcal{L}\left[\rho_{0,{\rm III}},\frac{\mathbf{q}_{0,{\rm III}}}{\sqrt{\rho_{0,{\rm III}}}},\mathbf{q}_{0,{\rm III}},\rho_{{\rm III}},\rho_{{\rm III}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{u}_{{\rm III}},W_{{\rm III}}\right]
on
\mathcal{X}_{5}
is a Radon measure;

(3)

Random variables
\left\{\bar{\rho}_{0,{\rm II}},\frac{\bar{\mathbf{q}}_{0,{\rm II}}}{\sqrt{\bar{\rho}_{0,{\rm II}}}},\bar{\mathbf{q}}_{0,{\rm II}},\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}},\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{{\rm II}},\bar{W}_{{\rm II}}\right\}

converges to

\left\{\rho_{0,{\rm III}},\frac{\mathbf{q}_{0,{\rm III}}}{\sqrt{\rho}_{0,{\rm III}}},\mathbf{q}_{0,{\rm III}},\rho_{{\rm III}},\rho_{{\rm III}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{u}_{{\rm III}},W_{{\rm III}}\right\}
in the topology of
\mathcal{X}_{5}
,
\bar{\mathbb{P}}

a.s.
as
n\rightarrow\infty
.

Step 2:
The estimates for the limit system.

Recall (
3.1
) and (
3.46
),
by Gagliardo-Nirenberg’s interpolation inequality, it holds

\delta^{\frac{9}{19}}\left|\nabla\triangle^{4}\bar{\rho}_{\delta}\right|\in L^{\frac{19}{9}}\left(0,T;L^{3}(\mathbb{T}^{3})\right)

[
39
]
.
Therefore, for any
\iota>0
, Chebyshev’s inequality shows this highest order derivative of
\rho
term goes to
0

\bar{\mathbb{P}}
almost everywhere. For all
\varphi(t)\in C_{c}^{\infty}\left([0,T)\right)
, and all
\psi(x)\in C^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{T}^{3}\right)
, we have

(4.62)

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left[\left\{\left|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\delta\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}\nabla\triangle^{9}\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}\varphi(t)\psi(x)\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}s\right|>\iota\right\}\right]

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle C\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\delta\triangle^{5}\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}\nabla\triangle^{4}\left(\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}\varphi(t)\psi(x)\right)\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}s\right|^{r}\right]}{\iota^{r}}.

Taking the highest order derivative of
\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}
for example, since the lower order terms are controlled by this term, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\delta\triangle^{5}\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}\nabla\triangle^{4}\left(\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}\right)\varphi(t)\psi(x)\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}s\right|^{r}\right]}{\iota^{r}}

(4.63)

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\left|C(\varphi,\psi)\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\Omega}\sqrt{\delta}\left|\triangle^{5}\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}\right|\delta^{\frac{9}{19}}\left|\nabla\triangle^{4}\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}\right|\delta^{\frac{1}{38}}\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}s\right|^{r}\right]}{\iota^{r}}

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle\frac{C(\varphi,\psi)\delta^{\frac{1}{38}}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\sqrt{\delta}\left\|\triangle^{5}\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}\right\|_{L_{t}^{2}L_{x}^{2}}\right]^{2r}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\delta^{\frac{9}{19}}\nabla\triangle^{4}\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}\right\|_{L_{t}^{\frac{19}{9}}L_{x}^{3}}\right]^{2r}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\iota^{r}}

\displaystyle\rightarrow 0\quad\text{ as }\delta\rightarrow 0.

In order to handle

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\delta\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}\nabla\triangle^{9}\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}\cdot\nabla_{\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{{\rm II}}}\varphi_{n}\left(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{{\rm II}}\right)\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}s\right|^{r}\right]

(4.64)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\delta\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}\sum\limits_{i=1}^{3}\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{i}}\triangle^{9}\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}\left(1+\ln\left(1+|\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{{\rm II}}|^{2}\right)\right)2\bar{u}_{{\rm II},i}\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}s\right|^{r}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 3^{r-1}\sum\limits_{i=1}^{3}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\delta\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{i}}\triangle^{9}\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}\left(1+\ln\left(1+|\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{{\rm II}}|^{2}\right)\right)2\bar{u}_{{\rm II},i}\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}s\right|^{r}\right],

we calculate

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\delta\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{1}}\triangle^{9}\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}\left(1+\ln\left(1+|\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{{\rm II}}|^{2}\right)\right)2\bar{u}_{{\rm II},1}\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}s\right|^{r}\right]

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle C_{n,r}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\delta\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}\left|\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{1}}\triangle^{9}\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}\right|\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}s\right|^{r}\right]

(4.65)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C_{n,r}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\left(I_{\{\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{1}}\triangle^{9}\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}>0\}}-I_{\{\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{1}}\triangle^{9}\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}\leqslant 0\}}\right)\delta\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{1}}\triangle^{9}\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}s\right|^{r}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C_{n,r}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}I_{\{\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{1}}\triangle^{9}\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}>0\}}\delta\triangle^{5}\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{1}}\triangle^{4}\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}s\right.\right.

\displaystyle\quad\quad\quad\left.\left.-\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}I_{\{\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{1}}\triangle^{9}\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}\leqslant 0\}}\delta\triangle^{5}\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{1}}\triangle^{4}\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}s\right|^{r}\right]

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle C_{n,r}\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\delta\left|\triangle^{5}\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}\right|\left|\frac{\partial}{\partial x_{1}}\triangle^{4}\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}\right|\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}s\right|^{r}\right],

where
C_{n,r}=\left(2(e(1+n)^{2}-1)\left(1+\ln n\right)+1\right)^{r}
. So we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\delta\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}\nabla\triangle^{9}\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}\cdot\nabla_{\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{{\rm II}}}\varphi_{n}\left(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{{\rm II}}\right)\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}s\right|^{r}\right]

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle 3^{r}C\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\delta\left|\triangle^{5}\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}\right|\left|\nabla\triangle^{4}\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}\right|\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}s\right)^{r}\right]

(4.66)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 3^{r}C\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\delta^{\frac{1}{38}}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\delta^{\frac{1}{2}}|\triangle^{5}\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}|\delta^{\frac{9}{19}}|\nabla\triangle^{4}\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}|\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}s\right)^{r}\right]

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle 3^{r}C\left(\delta^{\frac{1}{38}}\right)^{r}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\delta^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\|\triangle^{5}\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}\right\|_{L_{t}^{2}L_{x}^{2}}\right]^{2r}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\delta^{\frac{9}{19}}\nabla\triangle^{4}\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}\right\|_{L_{t}^{\frac{19}{9}}L_{x}^{3}}\right]^{2r}\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle 3^{r}C\left(\delta^{\frac{1}{38}}\right)^{r},

for any
\delta<n^{-\alpha}
,
\alpha>76
, where
C
depends on
r,T,\mathbb{E}\left[(e_{{\rm II}}(0))^{r}\right]
and
\mathbb{E}\left[(\tilde{e}_{{\rm II}}(0))^{r}\right]
.

\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\left|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\delta\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}\nabla\triangle^{9}\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}\nabla_{\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{{\rm II}}}\varphi_{n}\left(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{{\rm II}}\right)\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}t\right|\right)^{r}\right]\rightarrow 0
as
n\rightarrow\infty
.

Then, we consider the limit
\eta\rightarrow 0
. We claim that
\eta\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}^{-10}\operatorname{d}x\rightarrow 0
as
\eta\rightarrow 0
. In fact,

(4.67)

\left|\left\{x\mid\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}(t,x)=0\right\}\right|=0\quad\text{ for almost every }t,x,\omega.

By Aubin-Lion’s lemma, the strong convergence of
\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}
in
L^{2}(0,T;L^{1}\left(\mathbb{T}^{3}\right))
still holds, so
\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}^{-10}\rightarrow\rho_{{\rm III}}^{-10}
holds almost everywhere. So the
\eta\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}^{-10}\operatorname{d}x
in energy will vanish as
\eta\rightarrow 0
. Next, we deal with
\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\left|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\eta\nabla\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}^{-10}\nabla_{\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{{\rm II}}}\varphi_{n}\left(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{{\rm II}}\right)\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}s\right|\right)^{r}\right]
. Since

\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\eta\frac{\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}^{-10}}{10}\operatorname{d}x\right)^{r}\right]\leqslant C,

\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\eta\frac{11}{25}\left|\nabla\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}^{-5}\right|^{2}\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}s\right)^{r}\right]\leqslant C,

calculating

(4.68)

|\nabla\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}^{-10}|=2\left|\nabla\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}\right|\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}^{-11}=2\left|\nabla\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\right|\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}^{-10-\frac{1}{2}}.

by Hölder’s inequality, we have

(4.69)

\displaystyle\eta^{\frac{11}{20}}\left\|\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}^{-10-\frac{1}{2}}\right\|_{L_{t}^{1}L_{x}^{\frac{60}{23}}}\leqslant\eta^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\|\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}^{-10}\right\|_{L_{t}^{1}L_{x}^{3}}\eta^{\frac{1}{20}}\left\|\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}L_{x}^{20}}\leqslant C,

where
C
depends on
r,T,\mathbb{E}\left[(e_{{\rm II}}(0))^{r}\right]
and
\mathbb{E}\left[(\tilde{e}_{{\rm II}}(0))^{r}\right]
.
So by Hölder’s inequality, it holds

\displaystyle\eta^{\frac{11}{20}}\int_{0}^{t}\left|\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\nabla\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}^{-10}\operatorname{d}x\right|^{\frac{60}{23}}\operatorname{d}s=\eta^{\frac{11}{20}}\int_{0}^{t}\left|\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}^{-10-\frac{1}{2}}\left|\nabla\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\right|\operatorname{d}x\right|^{\frac{60}{23}}\operatorname{d}s

(4.70)

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle C\sup\limits_{s\in[0,T]}\left(\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\left|\nabla\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\right|^{2}\operatorname{d}x\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\int_{0}^{t}\eta^{\frac{11}{20}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}^{(-10-\frac{1}{2})\frac{60}{23}}\operatorname{d}x\right)^{\frac{23}{60}\frac{60}{23}}\operatorname{d}s

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle C\sup\limits_{s\in[0,T]}\left(\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\left|\nabla\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\right|^{2}\operatorname{d}x\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}\eta^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\|\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}^{-10}\right\|_{L_{t}^{1}L_{x}^{3}}\eta^{\frac{1}{20}}\left\|\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}^{-\frac{1}{2}}\right\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}L_{x}^{20}},

where
C
depends on the length of the period of the area.
So we conclude that
\eta^{\frac{253}{1200}}\nabla\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}^{-10}\in L^{\frac{60}{23}}\left(0,T;L^{1}\left(\mathbb{T}^{3}\right)\right)
. Next, we estimate

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\eta\nabla\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}^{-10}\nabla_{\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{{\rm II}}}\varphi_{n}\left(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{{\rm II}}\right)\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}s\right|^{r}\right]

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\left|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\eta|\nabla\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}^{-10}|\left(1+\ln\left(1+|\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{{\rm II}}|^{2}\right)\right)2|\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{{\rm II}}|\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}s\right|\right)^{r}\right]

(4.71)

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left(2(e(1+n)^{2}-1)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(1+\ln(1+n)\right)\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\eta\left|\nabla\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}^{-10}\right|\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}s\right)^{r}\right]

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left(2(e(1+n)^{2}-1)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(1+\ln(1+n)\right)\eta^{\frac{947}{1200}}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\eta^{\frac{253}{1200}}\left|\nabla\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}^{-10}\right|\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}s\right)^{r}\right]

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle C\mathbb{E}\left[\left(2(e(1+n)^{2}-1)^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(1+\ln(1+n)\right)\eta^{\frac{947}{1200}}\left\|\eta^{\frac{253}{1200}}\nabla\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}^{-10}\right\|_{L_{t}^{\frac{60}{23}}L_{x}^{1}}\right)^{r}\right],

for any
\eta<n^{-\beta}
,
\beta>\frac{2400}{947}
,
\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\left|\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\eta\nabla\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}^{-10}\nabla_{\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{{\rm II}}}\varphi_{n}\left(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{{\rm II}}\right)\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}t\right|\right)^{r}\right]\rightarrow 0
as
n\rightarrow\infty
.
For all
\varphi(t)\in C_{c}^{\infty}\left([0,T)\right)
, and all
\psi(x)\in C^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{T}^{3}\right)
, for any
\iota>0
, we obtain

\displaystyle\quad\mathbb{P}\left[\left\{\left|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\eta\nabla\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}^{-10}\varphi(t)\psi(x)\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}s\right|>\iota\right\}\right]

(4.72)

\displaystyle\leqslant\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\int_{0}^{t}\varphi(t)\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\eta\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}^{-10}\nabla\psi(x)\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}s\right|^{r}\right]}{\iota^{r}}\leqslant\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\int_{0}^{t}\varphi(t)\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\eta\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}^{-10}\nabla\psi(x)\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}s\right|^{r}\right]}{\iota^{r}}

\displaystyle\leqslant\eta^{\frac{r}{2}}\frac{\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\left\|\eta^{\frac{1}{2}}\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}^{-10}\right\|_{L_{t}^{2}L_{x}^{3}}\left\|\varphi(t)\right\|_{L_{t}^{2}}\left\|\psi(x)\right\|_{H_{x}^{10}}\right)^{r}\right]}{\iota^{r}}\leqslant C\eta^{\frac{r}{2}}\rightarrow 0\quad\text{as }\eta\rightarrow 0,

where
C
depends on
r,T,\mathbb{E}\left[(e_{{\rm II}}(0))^{r}\right]
and
\mathbb{E}\left[(\tilde{e}_{{\rm II}}(0))^{r}\right]
.

From (
2.3
), we have

(4.73)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}^{-1}\right\|^{r}_{L_{t}^{\infty}L_{x}^{\infty}}\right]

\displaystyle\leqslant C_{r}\eta^{-\frac{3}{10}r}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\eta^{r}\left\|\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}^{-1}\right\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}L_{x}^{10}}^{10r}\right]\right)^{\frac{3}{10}}\delta^{-r}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\delta^{r}\left\|\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}\right\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{x}^{9}}^{2r}\right)^{\frac{10}{7}}\right]\right)^{\frac{7}{10}}

\displaystyle\quad+C_{r}\eta^{-r}\mathbb{E}\left[\eta^{r}\left\|\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}^{-1}\right\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}L_{x}^{10}}^{10r}\right]^{\frac{1}{5}}\delta^{-\frac{1}{2}r}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\delta^{\frac{1}{2}r}\left\|\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}\right\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}H_{x}^{9}}^{r}\right)^{\frac{5}{4}}\right]^{\frac{4}{5}}.

We estimate the added damping term as follows

(4.74)

\displaystyle-r_{0}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}|\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{{\rm II}}|^{2}\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{{\rm II}}\cdot\nabla\ln\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}s\leqslant r_{0}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}^{\frac{3}{4}}|\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{{\rm II}}|^{2}\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{{\rm II}}|\frac{\nabla\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}}{\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}^{\frac{3}{4}}}|\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}s

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle r_{0}\left\|\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}^{-1}\right\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}L_{x}^{\infty}}^{\frac{7}{4}}\left\||\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}^{\frac{3}{4}}\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{{\rm II}}|^{2}\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{{\rm II}}\right\|_{L_{t}^{\frac{4}{3}}L_{x}^{\frac{4}{3}}}\left\|\nabla\triangle^{4}\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}\right\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}L_{x}^{2}}.

So for our setting,
\eta=n^{-\alpha}
,
\delta=n^{-\beta}
, roughly we choose
r_{0}=n^{-\left(1+\frac{7}{4}\left(\frac{13}{10}\alpha+\frac{3}{2}\beta\right)+\frac{\beta}{2}\right)}
, then

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}\left[\left|r_{0}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}|\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{{\rm II}}|^{2}\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{{\rm II}}\cdot\nabla\ln\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}s\right|^{r}\right]\leqslant r_{0}^{r}\left(\eta^{-\frac{3}{10}r}\delta^{-r}+\eta^{-r}\delta^{-\frac{1}{2}r}\right)^{\frac{7}{4}}\delta^{-\frac{r}{2}}C\rightarrow 0\text{ as }n\rightarrow\infty,

where
C
depends on
r,T,\mathbb{E}\left[(e_{{\rm II}}(0))^{r}\right]
and
\mathbb{E}\left[(\tilde{e}_{{\rm II}}(0))^{r}\right]
.
Therefore we take limit
r_{0}\rightarrow 0
, i.e.,
n\rightarrow\infty
, in the Stochastic B-D entropy estimates.

For passing to the limit
n\rightarrow\infty
, we still have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\left|\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}\mathbf{F}_{k}\nabla_{\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{{\rm II}}}\varphi_{n}\left(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{{\rm II}}\right)\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}\bar{W}_{{\rm II}}\right|\right)^{r}\right]

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\left|\int_{0}^{t}\left(\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}\mathbf{F}_{k}\nabla_{\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{{\rm II}}}\varphi_{n}\left(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{{\rm II}}\right)\operatorname{d}x\right)^{2}\operatorname{d}s\right|\right)^{\frac{r}{2}}\right]

(4.75)

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left(f_{k}^{2}\int_{0}^{t}\left(\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}(1+\ln(1+|\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{{\rm II}}|^{2}))2|\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{{\rm II}}|\operatorname{d}x\right)^{2}\operatorname{d}s\right)^{\frac{r}{2}}\right]

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left(f_{k}^{2}\int_{0}^{t}\left(\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}|\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{{\rm II}}|^{2}\operatorname{d}x\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}(1+\ln(1+|\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{{\rm II}}|^{2}))^{2}\operatorname{d}x\right)\operatorname{d}s\right)^{\frac{r}{2}}\right]

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left(f_{k}^{2}\left\|\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{{\rm II}}\right\|_{L_{s}^{\infty}L_{x}^{2}}^{2}\left(t\left\|\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}\right\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}L_{x}^{2}}+\left\|\left(\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}\right)^{\frac{1}{4}}\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{{\rm II}}\right\|_{L_{t}^{4}L_{x}^{4}}\right)\right)^{\frac{r}{2}}\right]\leqslant C,

where
C
depends on
r,T,\mathbb{E}\left[(e_{{\rm II}}(0))^{r}\right]
and
\mathbb{E}\left[(\tilde{e}_{{\rm II}}(0))^{r}\right]
. When
n\rightarrow+\infty
, for the stochastic part, since

(4.76)

\nabla_{\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{{\rm II}}}^{2}\varphi_{n}\left(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{{\rm II}}\right)\rightarrow\left(\left(2+\ln\left(1+|\mathbf{u}_{{\rm III}}|^{2}\right)\right)\mathbb{I}_{3}+4\frac{\rho_{{\rm III}}\mathbf{u}_{{\rm III}}\otimes\rho_{{\rm III}}\mathbf{u}_{{\rm III}}}{|\rho_{{\rm III}}\mathbf{u}_{{\rm III}}|^{2}}\right),

we gain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\int_{0}^{t}\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}\mathbb{F}(\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}},\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{{\rm II}})\nabla_{\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{{\rm II}}}^{2}\varphi_{n}\left(\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{{\rm II}}\right)\mathbb{F}(\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}},\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{{\rm II}})\operatorname{d}s\operatorname{d}x\right|^{r}\right]

(4.77)

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{k=1}^{+\infty}f_{k}^{2}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\int_{0}^{t}\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}|\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{{\rm II}}|^{2}\operatorname{d}s\operatorname{d}x\right|^{r}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\int_{0}^{t}\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}\operatorname{d}s\operatorname{d}x\right|^{r}\right]\right)\leqslant C,

where
C
depends on
r,T,\mathbb{E}\left[(e_{{\rm II}}(0))^{r}\right]
and
\mathbb{E}\left[(\tilde{e}_{{\rm II}}(0))^{r}\right]
. Hence we prove the following lemma.

Lemma 4.2
.

(Mellet-Vasseur’s inequality in the stochastic version) For
\varpi\in(0,2)
, where
\varpi
is small, for some
r>4
, it holds

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\rho_{{\rm III}}\left(1+|\mathbf{u}_{{\rm III}}|^{2}\right)\ln\left(1+|\mathbf{u}_{{\rm III}}|^{2}\right)\operatorname{d}x\right)^{r}\right]

(4.78)

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle\bar{C}+C_{r}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\rho_{0,{\rm III}}\left(1+\frac{\left|\mathbf{q}_{0,{\rm III}}\right|^{2}}{\rho_{0,{\rm III}}^{2}}\right)\ln\left(1+\frac{\left|\mathbf{q}_{0,{\rm III}}\right|^{2}}{\rho_{0,{\rm III}}^{2}}\right)\operatorname{d}x\right)^{r}\right]

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}\left[\left(8\left(\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\left(\frac{\left|\mathbf{q}_{0,{\rm III}}\right|^{2}}{\rho_{0,{\rm III}}}+\frac{\rho_{0,{\rm III}}^{\gamma}}{\gamma-1}+\left|\nabla\sqrt{\rho_{0,{\rm III}}}\right|^{2}\right)\operatorname{d}x+2e_{0,{\rm III}}\right)\right)^{r}\right]

\displaystyle+\mathbb{E}\left[\left(C\int_{0}^{t}\left(\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\left(\rho_{{\rm III}}^{2\gamma-1-\frac{\varpi}{2}}\right)^{\frac{2}{2-\varpi}}\right)^{\frac{2-\varpi}{2}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\rho_{{\rm III}}\left(2+\ln\left(1+|\mathbf{u}_{{\rm III}}|^{2}\right)\right)^{\frac{2}{\varpi}}\operatorname{d}x\right)^{\frac{\varpi}{2}}\operatorname{d}s\right)^{r}\right],

and there exists a constant
C
, such that

\left(\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\rho_{{\rm III}}\left(2+\ln\left(1+|\mathbf{u}_{{\rm III}}|^{2}\right)\right)^{\frac{2}{\varpi}}\operatorname{d}x\right)^{\frac{\varpi}{2}}\leqslant C\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\rho_{{\rm III}}\mathbf{u}_{{\rm III}}^{2}\operatorname{d}x,

\bar{C}
and
C_{r}
share the same meaning as in lemma
4.1
.

Proposition 4.5
.

\left(\left(\bar{\Omega},\bar{\mathcal{F}},\bar{\mathbb{P}}\right),\rho_{{\rm III}},\mathbf{u}_{{\rm III}},W_{{\rm III}}\right)
is a martingale solution to

(4.79)

\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\left(\rho_{{\rm III}}\right)_{t}+\operatorname{div}(\rho_{{\rm III}}\mathbf{u}_{{\rm III}})=0,\\
\operatorname{d}\left(\rho_{{\rm III}}\mathbf{u}_{{\rm III}}\right)+\left(\operatorname{div}(\rho_{{\rm III}}\mathbf{u}_{{\rm III}}\otimes\mathbf{u}_{{\rm III}})+\nabla\left(a\rho_{{\rm III}}^{\gamma}\right)-\operatorname{div}(\rho_{{\rm III}}\mathbb{D}\mathbf{u}_{{\rm III}})\right)\operatorname{d}t\\
=(-r_{1}\rho_{{\rm III}}|\mathbf{u}_{{\rm III}}|^{2}\mathbf{u}_{{\rm III}}-r_{2}\mathbf{u}_{{\rm III}})\operatorname{d}t+\rho_{{\rm III}}\mathbb{F}(\rho_{{\rm III}},\mathbf{u}_{{\rm III}})\operatorname{d}W_{{\rm III}}.\end{array}\right.

Proof.

The weak convergence of
\nabla\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}^{\frac{\gamma}{2}}
, still holds in the corresponding spaces. By Vitali’s convergence theorem,
\rho_{{\rm III}}
and
\mathbf{u}_{{\rm III}}
satisfy the mass conservation equation, it suffices to see whether the convergence of the term associated with stochastic forces holds or not in the momentum equation.
Since we still have the strong convergence of
\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}
in
L^{2}\left(0,T;L^{\frac{3}{2}}\left(\mathbb{T}^{3}\right)\right)
and the strong convergence of
\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{{\rm II}}
in
L^{2}\left([0,T]\times\mathbb{T}^{3}\right)
, hence
\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}\rightarrow\rho_{{\rm III}}
holds almost everywhere, and
\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{{\rm II}}\rightarrow\rho_{{\rm III}}\mathbf{u}_{{\rm III}}
holds almost everywhere up to a subsequence. Therefore
\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}\mathbb{F}(\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}},\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{{\rm II}})\rightarrow\rho_{{\rm III}}\mathbb{F}\left(\rho_{{\rm III}},\mathbf{u}_{{\rm III}}\right)
almost everywhere. By dominated convergence theorem, we have

(4.80)

\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}|\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}}\mathbb{F}(\bar{\rho}_{{\rm II}},\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{{\rm II}})|^{2}\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}t\rightarrow\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}|\rho_{{\rm III}}\mathbb{F}(\rho_{{\rm III}},\mathbf{u}_{{\rm III}})|^{2}\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}t,

\bar{\mathbb{P}}
a.s.
\square

We define the energy
e(t)=\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\left(\frac{1}{2}\rho_{{\rm III}}|\mathbf{u}_{{\rm III}}|^{2}+\frac{a}{\gamma}\rho_{{\rm III}}^{\gamma}\right)\operatorname{d}x
,
then the following lemma holds.

Lemma 4.3
.

For system (
4.79
), it holds

(1)

for some
r>\max\left\{6\gamma,\frac{4\gamma}{5\gamma-6}\right\}
,

(4.81)

\mathbb{E}\left[\sup\limits_{t\in[0,T]}e_{{\rm III}}(t)^{r}\right]\leqslant C\left(t^{\frac{r}{2}}+t^{r}+\mathbb{E}\left[e_{{\rm III}}(0)^{r}\right]\right)e^{C\left(t^{\frac{r}{2}}+t^{r}\right)}\leqslant C\left(\mathbb{E}\left[e_{{\rm III}}(0)^{r}\right]+1\right),

where
C
depends on
r

T
, and
\sum\limits_{k=1}^{+\infty}f^{2}_{k}
.

(2)

Let
\tilde{e}_{{\rm III}}=\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\left(\frac{1}{2}\rho_{{\rm III}}|\mathbf{u}_{{\rm III}}+\nabla\ln\rho_{{\rm III}}|^{2}+\int_{1}^{\rho_{{\rm III}}}\frac{p(z)}{z}\operatorname{d}z+r_{2}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\ln_{-}\rho_{{\rm III}}\right)\operatorname{d}x
, then we have

(4.82)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sup\limits_{t\in[0,T]}\tilde{e}_{{\rm III}}(t)^{r}\right]

\displaystyle\leqslant C,

(4.83)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left(r_{1}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\rho_{{\rm III}}|\mathbf{u}_{{\rm III}}|^{4}\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}s\right)^{r}\right]

\displaystyle\leqslant C,\quad\mathbb{E}\left[\left(r_{2}\int_{0}^{t}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}|\mathbf{u}_{{\rm III}}|^{2}\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}s\right)^{r}\right]\leqslant C,

where
C
depends on
r,T,\mathbb{E}\left[e_{{\rm III}}(0)^{r}\right],\mathbb{E}\left[e_{{\rm III}}(0)^{\frac{6r}{5}}\right]
and
\mathbb{E}\left[e_{{\rm III}}(0)^{k_{i}r}\right]
,
i=1,2,3
,
in which
k_{{\rm I}}
are specific constants.

4.4.
Vanishing the drag forces:
r_{1}\rightarrow 0,r_{2}\rightarrow 0

Step 1:
Choose the path space, and show the tightness of the laws and the convergence.

We study the convergence of
\rho_{{\rm III}}
.

(4.84)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\rho_{{\rm III}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\rho_{{\rm III}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\operatorname{div}\mathbf{u}_{{\rm III}}\right\|_{L_{t}^{2}L_{x}^{\frac{3}{2}}}^{r}\right]\leqslant\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\rho_{{\rm III}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}L_{x}^{6}}^{r}\left\|\rho_{{\rm III}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\operatorname{div}\mathbf{u}_{{\rm III}}\right\|_{L_{t}^{2}L_{x}^{2}}^{r}\right]\leqslant C,

together with

(4.85)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\nabla\rho_{{\rm III}}^{\frac{1}{2}}(\rho_{{\rm III}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{u}_{{\rm III}})\right\|_{L_{t}^{2}L_{x}^{1}}^{r}\right]\leqslant\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\nabla\rho_{{\rm III}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\right\|_{L_{t}^{\infty}L_{x}^{2}}^{r}\left\|\rho_{{\rm III}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{u}_{{\rm III}}\right\|_{L_{t}^{2}L_{x}^{2}}^{r}\right]\leqslant C,

implies

(4.86)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left\|\left(\rho_{{\rm III}}\right)_{t}\right\|_{L_{t}^{2}L_{x}^{1}}^{r}\right]\leqslant C,

uniformly in
r_{1},r_{2}
.

Calculating
\left|\nabla\rho_{{\rm III}}\right|=2\left|\nabla\rho_{{\rm III}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\right|\rho_{{\rm III}}^{\frac{1}{2}},

\rho_{{\rm III}}
is bounded in
L^{\infty}\left(0,T;W^{1,1}\left(\mathbb{T}^{3}\right)\right)
, by Aubin-Lion’s lemma, we know that the strong convergence of
\rho_{{\rm III}}
still holds in
L^{2}\left(0,T;L^{\frac{3}{2}}\left(\mathbb{T}^{3}\right)\right)
.

Take the space
\mathcal{X}_{6}=\mathcal{X}_{\rho_{0}}^{6}\times\mathcal{X}_{\frac{\mathbf{q}_{0}}{\sqrt{\rho_{0}}}}^{6}\times\mathcal{X}_{\rho}^{6}\times\mathcal{X}_{\rho^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{u}}^{6}\times\mathcal{X}_{W}^{6}
, where
\mathcal{X}_{\rho_{0}}^{6}=L^{\gamma}\left(\mathbb{T}^{3}\right)
,

\mathcal{X}_{\frac{\mathbf{q}_{0}}{\sqrt{\rho_{0}}}}^{6}=L^{2}\left(\mathbb{T}^{3}\right)
,
\mathcal{X}_{\rho}^{6}=L^{2}\left(0,T;L^{\frac{3}{2}}\left(\mathbb{T}^{3}\right)\right)
,

\mathcal{X}_{\rho^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{u}}^{6}=L^{2}\left([0,T]\times\mathbb{T}^{3}\right)
,
\mathcal{X}_{W}^{6}=C([0,T];\mathcal{H})
. Similarly, we have the following convergence.

Proposition 4.6
.

There exist two families of
\mathcal{X}_{6}
-valued Borel measurable random variables

\left\{\bar{\rho}_{0,{\rm III}},\frac{\bar{\mathbf{q}}_{0,{\rm III}}}{\sqrt{\bar{\rho}_{0,{\rm III}}}},\bar{\rho}_{{\rm III}},\bar{\rho}_{{\rm III}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{{\rm III}},\bar{W}_{{\rm III}}\right\}

and

\left\{\rho_{0},\frac{\mathbf{q}_{0}}{\sqrt{\rho_{0}}},\rho,\rho^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{u},W\right\}
,
defined on a new complete probability space
\left(\bar{\Omega},\bar{\mathcal{F}},\bar{\mathbb{P}}\right)
, such that (up to a sequence):

(1)

\mathcal{L}\left[\bar{\rho}_{0,{\rm III}},\frac{\bar{\mathbf{q}}_{0,{\rm III}}}{\sqrt{\bar{\rho}_{0,{\rm III}}}},\bar{\rho}_{{\rm III}},\bar{\rho}_{{\rm III}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{{\rm III}},\bar{W}_{{\rm III}}\right]
and

\mathcal{L}\left[\rho_{0,{\rm III}},\frac{\mathbf{q}_{0,{\rm III}}}{\sqrt{\rho_{0,{\rm III}}}},\rho_{{\rm III}},\rho_{{\rm III}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{u}_{{\rm III}},W_{{\rm III}}\right]

coincide with each other on
\mathcal{X}_{6}
;

(2)

\mathcal{L}\left[\rho_{0},\frac{\mathbf{q}_{0}}{\sqrt{\rho_{0}}},\rho,\rho^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{u},W\right]
on
\mathcal{X}_{6}
is a Radon measure;

(3)

The random variables
\left\{\bar{\rho}_{0,{\rm III}},\frac{\bar{\mathbf{q}}_{0,{\rm III}}}{\sqrt{\bar{\rho}_{0,{\rm III}}}},\bar{\rho}_{{\rm III}},\bar{\rho}^{\frac{1}{2}}\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{{\rm III}},\bar{W}_{{\rm III}}\right\}

converges
\bar{\mathbb{P}}

a.s.
to

\left\{\rho_{0},\frac{\mathbf{q}_{0}}{\sqrt{\rho_{0}}},\rho,\rho\mathbf{u},W\right\}
in the topology of
\mathcal{X}_{6}
.

Step 2:

\left(\left(\bar{\Omega},\bar{\mathcal{F}},\bar{\mathbb{P}}\right),\rho,\mathbf{u},W\right)
is a global martingale solution to our system (
1.7
), which finally proves our main theorem.

Lemma 4.4
.

The stochastic B-D entropy estimates become

(4.87)

\displaystyle\tilde{e}=\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\left(\frac{1}{2}\rho|\mathbf{u}+\nabla\ln\rho|^{2}+\int_{1}^{\rho}\frac{p(z)}{z}\operatorname{d}z\right)\operatorname{d}x,\quad\mathbb{E}\left[\sup\limits_{t\in[0,T]}\tilde{e}(t)^{r}\right]

\displaystyle\leqslant C,

where
C
depends on
r,T,\mathbb{E}\left[e(0)^{r}\right],\mathbb{E}\left[e(0)^{\frac{6r}{5}}\right]
and
\mathbb{E}\left[e(0)^{k_{{\rm I}}r}\right]
,
i=1,2,3
, in which
k_{{\rm I}}
are specific constants.

Lemma 4.5
.

\left(\left(\bar{\Omega},\bar{\mathcal{F}},\bar{\mathbb{P}}\right),\rho,\mathbf{u},W\right)
is a global-in-time martingale solution to system (
1.7
).

Proof.

The proof is similar to the last section. We just show that the terms involving
r_{1}\bar{\rho}_{{\rm III}}\left|\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{{\rm III}}\right|^{2}\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{{\rm III}}
and
r_{2}\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{{\rm III}}
tend to zero in distribution sense as
r_{2}\rightarrow 0
and
r_{1}\rightarrow 0

\bar{\mathbb{P}}
a.s. For
r_{1}\bar{\rho}_{{\rm III}}\left|\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{{\rm III}}\right|^{2}\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{{\rm III}}
, for all
\varphi(t)\in C_{c}^{\infty}\left([0,T)\right)
, and all
\psi(x)\in C^{\infty}\left(\mathbb{T}^{3}\right)
, we have

(4.88)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}r_{1}\bar{\rho}_{{\rm III}}\left|\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{{\rm III}}\right|^{2}\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{{\rm III}}\varphi(t)\psi(x)\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}t

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle r_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\|r_{1}^{\frac{1}{2}}\bar{\rho}_{{\rm III}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\left|\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{{\rm III}}\right|^{2}\right\|_{L_{t}^{2}L_{x}^{2}}\left\|\bar{\rho}_{{\rm III}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\ \bar{\mathbf{u}}_{{\rm III}}\right\|_{L_{t}^{2}L_{x}^{2}}\|\varphi(t)_{L_{t}^{2}}\|\psi(x)\|_{L_{x}^{\infty}}\rightarrow 0

as
r_{1}\rightarrow 0
.
The term
r_{2}\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{{\rm III}}

(4.89)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}r_{2}\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{{\rm III}}\varphi(t)\psi(x)\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}t\leqslant\int_{0}^{T}\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}r_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}r_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}\left|\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{{\rm III}}\varphi(t)\psi(x)\right|\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}t

\displaystyle\leqslant

\displaystyle r_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\|r_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}\bar{\mathbf{u}}_{{\rm III}}\right\|_{L_{t}^{2}L_{x}^{2}}\|\varphi(t)\|_{L_{t}^{2}}\|\psi(x)\|_{L_{x}^{2}}\rightarrow 0

as
r_{2}\rightarrow 0
.
Letting
r_{1}\rightarrow 0
and
r_{2}\rightarrow 0
, we obtains that

\displaystyle-\varphi(0)\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\mathbf{q}_{0}\cdot\psi(x)\operatorname{d}x-\int_{0}^{T}\varphi_{t}(t)\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\rho\mathbf{u}\psi(x)\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}t

(4.90)

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{T}\varphi(t)\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\rho\mathbf{u}\otimes\mathbf{u}:\nabla\psi(x)\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}t-\int_{0}^{T}\varphi(t)\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\rho^{\gamma}\operatorname{div}\psi(x)\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}t

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{T}\varphi(t)\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\rho\mathbb{D}\mathbf{u}:\nabla\psi(x)\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}t=\int_{0}^{T}\varphi(t)\int_{\mathbb{T}^{3}}\rho\mathbb{F}(\rho,\mathbf{u})\psi(x)\operatorname{d}x\operatorname{d}W,

\bar{\mathbb{P}}
a.s.
\square

5.
Appendix: Some preliminaries in stochastic analysis

In this section, for the convenience of the reader, we list some basic definitions and theorems in measure theory and stochastic analysis.

5.1.
Definitions

We first list some definitions.

1.

Stochastic basis.
Let
\left(\Omega,\mathcal{F},\mathbb{P}\right)
be a stochastic basis. A filtration
\mathcal{F}=\left(\mathcal{F}_{t}\right)_{t\in\mathbf{T}}
is a family of
\sigma
-algebras on
\Omega
indexed by
\mathbf{T}
such that
\mathcal{F}_{s}\subseteq

\mathcal{F}_{t}\subseteq\mathcal{F}
,
s\leq t
,
s,t\in\mathbf{T}
. A measurable space, together with a filtration, is called a filtered space.

2.

Adapted stochastic process.
A stochastic process
X
is
\mathcal{F}
-adapted if
X_{t}
is
\mathcal{F}_{t}
-measurable for every
t\in\mathbf{T}
;

3.

Wiener Process.
An
\mathbb{R}^{m}
-valued stochastic process
W
is called an
\mathcal{F}
-adapted Wiener process, provided:

W(t)
is
\mathcal{F}_{t}
-adapted;
W(0)=0,\quad\mathbb{P}
a.s.;
W
has continuous trajectories:
t\mapsto W(t)
is continuous
\mathbb{P}
a.s.;
W
has independent increments:
W(t)-W(s)
is independent of
\mathcal{F}_{s}
for all
0\leq s\leq

t<\infty
.

4.

Stopping time.
On
\left(\Omega,\mathcal{F},\mathbb{P}\right)
, a random time is a measurable mapping
\tau:\Omega\rightarrow\mathbf{T}\cup\infty
. A random time is a stopping time if
\{\tau\leq t\}\in\mathcal{F}_{t}
for every
t\in\mathbf{T}
.
For a process
X
and a subset
V
of the state space we define the hitting time of
X
in
V
as

(5.1)

\displaystyle\tau_{V}(\omega)=\inf\left\{\left.t\in\mathbf{T}\right|X_{t}(\omega)\in V\right\}

If
X
is a continuous adapted process and
V
is closed, then
\tau_{V}
is a stopping time.

5.

Martingale.
A process
X
is called a martingale if

X
is adapted;

X_{t}
is integrable for every
t\in\mathbf{T}
;

X_{s}=\mathbb{E}\left[X_{t}\mid\mathcal{F}_{s}\right]
whenever
s\leq t,s,t\in\mathbf{T}
.

6.

Progressive measurability.
Let
\left(\Omega,\mathcal{F},\mathbb{P}\right)
be a filtrated space. Stochastic process
X
is progressively measurable or simply progressively measurable, if for
\omega\in\Omega
,
(\omega,s)\mapsto X(s,\omega),s\leq t
is
\mathcal{F}_{t}\otimes\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{T}\cap[0,t])
-measurable for every
t\in\mathbf{T}
, where
\mathcal{B}(\mathbf{T}\cap[0,t])
is Borel-algebra.
In particular, progressively measurable processes are automatically adapted. The reciprocal is true if the paths of the process are regular enough. Let
X
be right-continuous or left-continuous
\mathcal{F}
-adapted process. Then
X
is progressively measurable. Any measurable and adapted process admits a progressive modification.

7.

Stochastic integral.
By
\mathcal{M}_{c}^{2}
we denote the space of square integrable continuous martingales
M
such that
M_{0}=0
. For
M
in
\mathcal{M}_{c}^{2}
, we denote by
\langle M\rangle
the continuous natural process of bounded variation in the Doob-Meyer decomposition of the sub-martingale
M^{2}
, that is,
M^{2}-\langle M\rangle
is a martingale. We call
\langle M\rangle
the quadratic variations of
M
. Let
\mathcal{L}^{2}(M)
be the space of progressively measurable process
H
such that
\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{t}H^{2}\operatorname{d}\langle M\rangle\right]<+\infty
. There exists a unique continuous linear functional
\int\cdot\operatorname{d}M:\mathcal{L}^{2}(M)\rightarrow

\mathcal{M}_{2}^{c}
coinciding with the elementary stochastic integral on
\mathcal{S}
and for which holds

(5.2)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{0}^{t}H\operatorname{d}M\right)^{2}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{t}H^{2}\operatorname{d}\langle M\rangle\right].

Furthermore, the following properties hold

•

Linearity:
\int(\alpha H+\beta G)\operatorname{d}M=\alpha\int H\operatorname{d}M+\beta\int G\operatorname{d}M
for
\alpha
and
\beta
constants;

•

Stopping property:
\int 1_{\{\cdot\leq\tau\}}H\operatorname{d}M=\int H\operatorname{d}M^{\tau}=\int_{0}^{\cdot\wedge\tau}H\operatorname{d}M
;

•

Itô-Isometry: for every
t
,

\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{0}^{t}H\operatorname{d}M\right)^{2}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{0}^{t}H^{2}\operatorname{d}\langle M\rangle\right].

8.

Semi-Martingale.
A semi-martingale is a process
X
with decomposition
X=X_{0}+M+A
, where
A
is the difference of two increasing continuous processes and
M
is a continuous local martingale. For two semi-martingales
X=X_{0}+M+A
and
Y=Y_{0}+N+B
, we define

•

the quadratic variations:
\langle X\rangle\triangleq\langle M\rangle
;

•

the co-variations:
\langle X,Y\rangle\triangleq(\langle X+Y\rangle-\langle X-Y\rangle)/4=(\langle M+N\rangle-\langle M-N\rangle)/4=\langle M,N\rangle
.

For every progressive process
H
such that
\int_{0}^{t}|H|\operatorname{d}|A|<\infty
and
\int_{0}^{t}|H|^{2}\operatorname{d}\langle M\rangle<\infty
a.s. for every
t
, we therefore define

(5.3)

\displaystyle\int H\operatorname{d}X\triangleq\int H\operatorname{d}M+\int H\operatorname{d}A.

9.

Radon measure.
Let
\mathcal{M}
be a measure on the
\sigma
-algebra with its element belonging to a Hausdorff topological space
S
. The measure
\mathcal{M}
is called inner regular or tight if, for any open set
U
,

(5.4)

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}(U)=\sup_{K\subseteqq U}\{\mathcal{M}(K):K\quad is\quad compact\}.

The measure
\mathcal{M}
is called outer regular if for any Borel set
B
,

(5.5)

\displaystyle\mathcal{M}(B)=\inf_{B\subseteqq U}\{\mathcal{M}(U):U\quad is\quad open\}.

The measure
\mathcal{M}
is called locally finite if every point of
S
has a neighborhood
U
for which
\mathcal{M}(U)
is finite.
The measure
\mathcal{M}
is called a Radon measure if it is inner regular, outer regular, and locally finite.
A measure on
\mathbb{R}
is a Radon measure if and only if it is a locally finite Borel measure. Since a probability measure is globally finite, hence locally finite, so every probability measure on a Radon space is also a Radon measure. In particular, a separable complete metric space
(S,\mathcal{M})
is a Radon space. Gaussian measure on Euclidean space
\mathbb{R}^{n}
with its Borel
\sigma
-algebra is a Radon measure.

10.

Law of a random variable.
Let
(S,\mathcal{S})
be a measurable space. An
S
-valued random variable is a measurable mapping

\mathbf{U}:(\Omega,\mathcal{F})\rightarrow(S,\mathcal{S})
. We denote by
\mathcal{L}[\mathbf{U}]
or also
\mathcal{L}_{S}[\mathbf{U}]
the law of
\mathbf{U}
on
S
, that is,
\mathcal{L}[\mathbf{U}]
is the push-forward probability measure on
(S,\mathcal{S})
given by

(5.6)

\mathcal{L}\left[\mathbf{U}\right](A)=\mathbb{P}\left[\left\{\mathbf{U}\in A\right\}\right],\quad A\in\mathcal{S}.\\

In measure theory, a pushforward measure is obtained by using a measurable function, transferring a measure from one measurable space to another space.

5.2.
Theorems

In the paper, the following theorems are employed.

1.

Chebyshev’s inequality.
Let
X
be a random variable in probability space
\left(\Omega,\mathcal{F},\mathbb{P}\right)
,
\varepsilon>0
. For every
0<r<\infty
, Chebyshev’s inequality reads

(5.7)

\mathbb{P}\left[\left\{|X|\geq\varepsilon\right\}\right]\leq\frac{1}{\varepsilon^{r}}\mathbb{E}\left[|X|^{r}\right].

2.

Jakubowski’s extension of Skorokhod’s representation theorem.
[
34
]
[
23
]
Let
(S,\mathcal{M})
be a sub-Polish space and let
\mathcal{S}
be the
\sigma
-field generated by
\left\{f_{n};n\in\mathbb{N}\right\}
. If
\left(\mu_{n}\right)_{n\in\mathbb{N}}
is a tight sequence of probability measures on
(S,\mathcal{S})
, then there exists a subsequence
\left(n_{k}\right)
and
S
-valued Borel measurable random variables
\left(\mathbf{U}_{k}\right)_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
and
\mathbf{U}
defined on the standard probability space
([0,1],\overline{\mathcal{B}([0,1])},\bar{\mathbb{P}})
, such that
\mu_{n_{k}}
is the law of
\mathbf{U}_{k}
and
\mathbf{U}_{k}(\omega)
converges to
\mathbf{U}(\omega)
in
S
for every
\omega\in[0,1]
. Moreover, the law of
\mathbf{U}
is a Radon measure.

3.

Burkholder-Davis-Gundy’s inequality.

[
9
]
Let
M
be a continuous local martingale. Let
M^{\ast}=\max\limits_{0\leqslant s\leqslant t}|M(s)|
, for any
m>0
, then there exist constants
K^{m}
and
K_{m}
such that

(5.8)

K_{m}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\langle M\rangle_{T}\right)^{m}\right]\leqslant\mathbb{E}\left[\left(M^{\ast}_{T}\right)^{2m}\right]\leqslant K^{m}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\langle M\rangle_{T}\right)^{m}\right],

for every stopping time
T
. For
m\geq 1
,
K^{m}=\left(\frac{2m}{2m-1}\right)^{\frac{2m(2m-2)}{2}}
, which is equivalent to
e^{m}
as
m\rightarrow\infty
.

4.

Kolmogorov-Centov’s continuity theorem.
[
27
]
Let
(\Omega,\mathcal{F},\mathbb{P})
be a probability space and
\bar{X}
a process on
[0,T]
. Suppose that

(5.9)

\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\bar{X}_{t}-\bar{X}_{s}\right|^{\alpha}\right]\leq C|t-s|^{1+\beta},

for every
s<t\leq T
and some strictly positive constants
\alpha,\beta
and
C
. Then
\bar{X}
admits a continuous modification
X
,
\mathbb{P}\left[\left\{X_{t}=\bar{X}_{t}\right\}\right]=1
for every
t
, and
X
is locally Hölder continuous for every exponent
0<\gamma<\frac{\beta}{\alpha},
namely,

(5.10)

\mathbb{P}\left[\left\{\omega\left|\sum_{0<t-s<h(\omega),t,s\leq T}\frac{\left|X_{t}(\omega)-X_{s}(\omega)\right|}{|t-s|^{\gamma}}\leq\delta\right.\right\}\right]=1,

where
h(\omega)
is an strictly positive random variable a.s. and the constant
\delta>0
.

5.

Kolmogorov’s theorem.

[
27
]
The sequence of probability measures
\left\{\mathbb{P}_{n}\right\}_{n=1}^{\infty}
on

\left(C[0,\infty),\mathcal{B}\left(C[0,\infty)\right)\right)
,
is tight if and only if

(5.11)

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{\lambda\rightarrow\infty}\sup_{n\geqslant 1}\mathbb{P}_{n}\left[\left\{\left.\omega\right||\omega(0)|>\lambda\right\}\right]=0,

(5.12)

\displaystyle\lim\limits_{\delta\rightarrow 0}\sup_{n\geqslant 1}\mathbb{P}_{n}\left[\left\{\omega\left|\max\limits_{|s-t|\leqslant\delta}|\omega(s)-\omega(t)|>\varepsilon\right.\right\}\right]=0;\quad 0\leqslant s,t\leqslant T;\quad\forall T>0,\varepsilon>0.

6.

Itô’s formula.

[
22
]
Let
X
and
Y
be semi-martingales, then it holds

(5.13)

\displaystyle XY

\displaystyle=X_{0}Y_{0}+\int X\operatorname{d}Y+\int Y\operatorname{d}X+\int\operatorname{d}\langle X,Y\rangle

\displaystyle=X_{0}Y_{0}+\int X\operatorname{d}Y+\int Y\operatorname{d}X+\langle X,Y\rangle,

\langle X,Y\rangle
means the co-variation of
X
and
Y
. In particular, this formula can be used to calculate integration by part. More precisely, if
X
is continuous, then
\langle\operatorname{d}X,\operatorname{d}Y\rangle=0
and it holds
\int X\operatorname{d}Y=-\int Y\operatorname{d}X+XY-X_{0}Y_{0}
.

Data Availability Statement
Data sharing is not applicable to this article as no data sets were generated or analysed during the current study.
